# Agent log

Newest entry on top. One entry per run: date, chunk, what changed, commit,
what is next, defaults applied.

Older entries are in [`log/`](log/): [20–25 November 2026](log/2026-11-20-to-25.md), [1–19 November 2026](log/2026-11-01-to-19.md) and [October 2026](log/2026-10.md).

## 2026-11-28 · chat · closing the pilot, PR 20: a backup of the files, and a restore written step by step

- On chat/backup-files from origin/main ef7f7a30 (#293). The plan's PR 20 only (section 1, C): a backup of the files Storage keeps, a restore of it into a temporary project, and docs/production.md's B8 step by step. No migration, no new npm package, no workflow, nothing from a later PR. No SMS, push or email; nothing run against any real project; no customer named.
- **Why**: Supabase's backups page says the database's backups hold only the metadata of Storage's objects, not the objects, and that restoring an old backup doesn't bring back objects deleted after it; its "Restore to a New Project" page says only the storage metadata is copied. So the files need a backup of their own.
- **`tooling/backup-files.mjs [folder]`** (Node 20, Node's own fetch and crypto, nothing installed): the buckets the migrations create (`avatars`, `list-photos`, `chat-files`, `documents`, as the plan said; `BUCKETS`, kept equal to every `insert into storage.buckets` by the test), each refused if the project lacks it (a wrong project, or the publishable key, which sees no bucket); every folder listed page by page (100 a page, `POST /storage/v1/object/list/<bucket>` as storage-js calls it, a full page with nothing new stops it rather than going round), each file downloaded (three tries; a 400 or 404 once) into a new dated folder, `files-YYYY-MM-DD-HHmm`, under the folder given or `conceptapp-backups` in the user's folder, each file named by its place in the manifest, never its path. A file that never came is counted on the screen and listed inside the backup (`missing`), and the command ends with 1. The manifest has each file's bucket, path, size, content type, cache-control and SHA-256, the source project's ref (or `local`), the buckets' settings and the time. Then one file, `files-…backup`, beside the folder: a 1 KB header (JSON padded with spaces: format, version, `aes-256-gcm`, scrypt's N, r and p, a random 16-byte salt and 12-byte IV, the tag, and an HMAC check of the passphrase), then AES-256-GCM over the manifest's length, the manifest and each file, with every header field but the tag and check as AAD. scrypt is 2^17, 8, 1 (128 MiB, a third of a second on Node 20 here); 64 bytes, one half for AES, the other for the check; the passphrase NFC-normalised, at least 12 characters, typed twice. Each file is read back from the folder and checked by its SHA-256 as it is encrypted, the file is opened again from the disk to its end (the passphrase's check, every SHA-256, the tag, the manifest's digest the same as written), and only then is the plain folder deleted; it is deleted when anything fails too, with a half-written backup, and on Ctrl+C (`SIGINT`, `cleanUp`). An existing backup of the same minute is never written over.
- **Never inside the repo**: `folderRefusal` reads the folder both ways a path can be read, `..` from the words first (as Windows does) and segment by segment with every link followed and `..` taken from where the link went (as POSIX does), the existing part made real with `realpathSync.native`, and refuses when either lands in this repo (case folded on Windows and macOS), or in any folder with a `.git` above it (any checkout, worktrees too), or goes through a link that leads nowhere. Asked before anything is made or downloaded, and again on the created folder's real path.
- **The address and the key** from `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` in the session or a question that shows nothing as it is typed (raw mode: no letters, no stars; backspace; Ctrl+C stops; refused without a terminal, so never from a piped file), never from a file and never printed. The address must be `https://<ref>.supabase.co` or the local stack's loopback; the key `sb_secret_…` or the legacy service_role JWT, and the publishable key is named as such. The key goes only in the `apikey` and `authorization` headers, never in an address; Storage's errors are kept to their status. The screen gets counts and sizes per bucket, never a file's name, the address or the key; anything unexpected prints one fixed sentence, never the error.
- **`tooling/restore-files.mjs <file> [--overwrite]`**: the passphrase first, the backup read to its end (check, every SHA-256, tag) and what it holds said in counts, before any question about a project. Then conceptapp-dev's ref (the repo never writes it: it is the `SUPABASE_PROJECT_REF` secret of A1, "המזהה שמופיע בכתובת שלו בלוח", so the command asks for it) and production's when there is one (Enter when not), then the temporary project's ref twice, refused if the two differ, if it is no ref, or if it is dev's, production's or the backup's own source (`local` goes back to the local stack, for the demo), then that project's secret key, hidden. The project must have the app's buckets; a file already there stops everything before the first upload unless `--overwrite` (a project restored from the database's backup has every file's row and none of the files, so the drill needs it). Then the backup is read a second time, its manifest's digest must equal the first reading's, and each file is uploaded only once its SHA-256 matched, with its content type and cache-control; a failed upload is counted, the rest go on, and it ends with 1. Nothing is written to the disk.
- **Both** refuse to run where `CI` or `GITHUB_ACTIONS` is set (exit 2, nothing asked), and no workflow names either. `@supabase/supabase-js` is not used: 2.119.1's `createClient` throws on Node 20.11 ("Node.js detected but native WebSocket not found", its realtime part, tried here) and warns that Node 20 support ends, so a Dependabot bump could break Orel's command with no CI to see it; the two call Storage's REST API the way storage-js 2.119.1 does (its endpoints read in node_modules), with each path segment encoded.
- **docs/production.md B8**, in its own style: why (Supabase's words on Storage), only Orel and never GitHub; (1) the daily backup in `Database → Backups` (Pro keeps 7 days; PITR about 100 dollars a month for 7 days, per Supabase's page, "לבדוק"); (2) the files' backup, its four questions, the passphrase into the password manager first, never typed on the command line or as a PowerShell variable (PowerShell keeps a history file), where the file lands, BitLocker, the last three kept; (3) when: monthly, and before every release whose line says "כולל שינוי במבנה", before Migrate conceptapp-prod; (4) the drill: "Restore to a New Project" (Orel's account step, paid plans only, billed by the hour while it exists, "לבדוק"), its cron jobs off first (`cron.alter_job` for every job: Supabase says they start as soon as the restore completes), one counts query on both projects (every table's rows by `query_to_xml`, `storage.objects` included, no person's data), the files restored with `--overwrite` and what to check, and the project deleted; (5) a real restore to production is not this command's: a session first. "מה הקוד כבר עושה" has a row for both. `tooling/production-doc.test.ts`: B8's two PowerShell blocks exactly (`git pull`, then the command), Supabase's sentence, monthly and before a release with a structure change, the drill's words, the two SQL blocks, and every script's CI refusal and no workflow naming it.
- Tests. `tooling/backup-files.test.ts` (43): the buckets equal the migrations'; the folder refused in the repo, below it, by a relative path, by `..` out and back in, through a link (a junction on Windows) and through a link and then `..` (fails with the words alone: tried), in another checkout, through a dangling link, by case on Windows and macOS, and allowed outside, through a link too; the backup refused there before any call; addresses and keys; the REST calls (paths encoded, the key in the two headers and in no address, the status alone in errors); paging (230 files in pages of 100, nested folders, a stuck listing stops); a backup's tree (one file, no plain path or content in it), its manifest's fields and SHA-256s, its lines (counts only), a file that won't download (three tries, one for a gone file), no buckets, a failure half way (nothing left), an existing backup kept, an empty file and an empty project; the header's fields, the default scrypt, a new salt and IV each time; the wrong passphrase, a byte changed (three places), the tag changed, cut short, added to, the salt changed, N out of range, not a backup, too short; a file not matching its SHA-256 (none handed on after it); the hidden question (nothing echoed, backspace, Ctrl+C, no terminal); the command (four hidden questions, the session's settings, CI and GitHub Actions, folders in the repo, a wrong address, the publishable key, a short or mistyped passphrase, files missing, arguments). `tooling/restore-files.test.ts` (17): typed twice, the shape, dev, production, the source, `local`; every file back with its type and cache, nothing overwritten without `--overwrite`, missing buckets, a failed upload, the file swapped between the two readings (nothing uploaded), the held lines; the command (the order of questions, the wrong passphrase or a SHA-256 that doesn't match asking nothing about a project, each refused project asking no key, the publishable key, `--overwrite`, CI, arguments, a failed upload). `tooling/fake-storage.ts` is the in-memory Storage both use; no test touches the network.
- Docs: README (Production), ROADMAP (P9's row, "מה לא נבדק"), this entry.
- Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check`; on Node 20.11, both scripts imported, a backup and restore round trip with an in-memory storage (233 files, the wrong passphrase, a changed byte, a second restore refused), `storageClient` against a loopback `node:http` stand-in of Storage's API (real fetch: the URLs, the two headers, the upload's headers, a 409 kept to its status), and both commands without a terminal, in CI, inside the repo and without arguments. Not run here: against the local stack (Docker) or any real project, the questions in a real PowerShell window (raw mode, pasting, Hebrew), `pnpm build` (no route or config changed), e2e and pgTAP (CI).
- Checked against the code at ef7f7a30 (followed the code): (1) the buckets are the plan's four, from `20261107000200_profile_photos.sql`, `20261110001500_list_photos.sql`, `20261111000500_chat_files.sql` and `20261111000700_documents.sql`, none public; (2) the plan's "with the existing `@supabase/supabase-js`" doesn't hold on Node 20.11 (above), so Node's fetch; (3) the repo never names conceptapp-dev's ref (A1's `SUPABASE_PROJECT_REF` secret, and `production-doc.test.ts` keeps development's secret names out of every section but A1), so the restore asks for it; (4) B8 was a plan only, as the plan said; (5) Supabase's docs settled the plan's "לבדוק" on Storage: not in the database's backup.
- Defaults applied, to ask Orel: (1) the restore never writes to production (the task's rule); a real loss goes through a session and a temporary project first. Alternative: a production mode with its own guard, written when it is needed. (2) conceptapp-dev's and production's refs are asked each time, not read from settings. (3) The backup goes to `conceptapp-backups` in the user's folder (alternative: an external disk or a storage account, an account step). How long backups are kept (after review, asked once with the place): "כמה זמן שומרים במחשב שלך גיבויים של הקבצים? א. שלושת האחרונים: כשמגבים גם לפני כל שחרור עם שינוי במבנה, זה בערך שלושה שבועות אחורה. ב. הגיבוי של כל חודש משלושת החודשים האחרונים, ועוד האחרון, גם כשהוא מלפני שחרור (מומלץ). ג. רק החודש האחרון: קבצים שנמחקו נשארים פחות זמן, אבל אפשר להחזיר פחות." Applied: ב, written in B8 step 2. It also sets how long a deleted person's file lives outside the app, so the answer goes into PR 21's templates for the lawyer (B1). (4) When the restore drill runs (after review): "שחזור הניסיון של הגיבוי: מתי עושים אותו? א. פעם אחת לפני העסק. אז עוד אין קבצים, ולכן הוא בודק רק שהפקודות עובדות. ב. פעם לפני העסק, ועוד פעם אחרי החודש הראשון, כשיש כבר מסמכים ותמונות אמיתיים. זה עוד כשעה של פרויקט זמני (מומלץ). ג. כל חצי שנה." Applied: ב, written in B8 step 4 with its cost "לבדוק". (5) A file that won't download makes a partial backup that says so and ends with 1, rather than no backup. (6) The documents screen still says "אין להם עדיין גיבוי נוסף" (`app.chat.documents.notice.body`): true until Orel runs the monthly backup; a later PR can change it once he does. (7) The passphrase may be the same every month, as long as it is in the password manager.
- Next: Orel's B8 when production exists (B2); before then, a session can run the demo against the local stack (`pnpm exec supabase start`, then both commands with `http://127.0.0.1:54321`); then the plan's next PR.
- After review (seventeen findings, fourteen issues once the same ones are put together, all real once read in the code; each fixed the smallest way that holds): (1) **A plain folder that wouldn't delete turned a checked backup into "nothing saved, the folder deleted"** (two findings). The `rm` in `finally` had no retries, so an EBUSY (a file Windows still held) replaced the result, skipped the success, left the folder, and printed "אין תשובה מהפרויקט (EBUSY)". Now each deletion goes through `removeAll` (`maxRetries` 5, `retryDelay` 200, never throws, `existsSync` says what happened), the half-written file's too, so the error said is the one that stopped it; `backup()` returns `plainLeft` and keeps `work.plain`, and `main` says the encrypted file was checked, prints the folder's path on its own line with "Shift+Delete" (`plainLeftLine`), never "הכול תקין", and ends with 1; on a failure it says "ולא נשאר במחשב אף קובץ גלוי" only when that holds. `STOPPED` no longer claims the folder went. (2) **A window closed half way left the plain files for good** (three findings). `cleanUpOnStop` handles SIGINT, SIGHUP (Windows' closed console, about ten seconds), SIGBREAK (Windows only) and SIGTERM alike, once, and says where a folder is if it stays (`cleanUp` returns it). And every run first deletes the plain folders an earlier run left (a crash, a power cut): `files-YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM` folders holding only `objects/0000001.bin`… (anything else left alone), saying how many (`sweptLine`); one that won't go stops the run before any download, with its path (`Refusal("leftover", path)`, `leftoverLine`, exit 2). (3) **The plain files were staged in the folder named, maybe a synced one**: they now go to `conceptapp-backup-work` in the system's temp folder (`defaultStaging`; AppData\Local\Temp on Windows, which no sync client touches), checked like the named folder before and after it is made; only the encrypted file is written to the folder named. The plain `manifest.json`, every path and never read, is no longer written. A line before the quiet part (`SEALING`) says not to close the window. (4) **Offset paging could skip a file when one before it was deleted elsewhere**: each bucket is listed again once its files are down, and what the first listing missed (or what was added) is backed up. (5) **The address was typed hidden and the project never shown**: asked with `askVisible`, and `[i] הפרויקט: <ref>` printed before the download and in the summary; B8's check compares it with conceptapp-prod's. (6) **Disk errors read as the project not answering, and the restore said files may have gone up before any did**: `storageWords` says "הבעיה במחשב שלך ולא בפרויקט" for a file-system error (its own `syscall`, or ENOSPC, EACCES, EPERM, EEXIST, EBUSY and the like, never fetch's error, whose code is in `cause`); `restoreInto` counts uploads begun (`progress`), and the "--overwrite" advice comes only after one. (7) **A production backup could go to the local stack**: `local` only for a backup of `local` (`TARGET_WORDS.local`). (8) **conceptapp-dev was kept out only by a typed ref**: a hosted target that lists none of the backup's paths is refused (`unrelated`, `mustKnow`), even with `--overwrite`, whatever was typed for dev; a copy restored from the database's backup lists them. Edge, said on the screen: a drill whose database backup is older than every file in the files' backup is refused too, and goes to a session. (9) **The weekly release had no backup step**: step 0 of "עדכון אחרי שעסק אמיתי עלה" backs up the files first when the line says "כולל שינוי במבנה", its time a quarter hour, half with a structure change; B8 step 3 points to it and adds a monthly calendar reminder. (10) **The only drill ran with no files** (two findings): B8 step 4 now says so and runs it twice, again after the first month, with documents and avatars not 0 as the check; 4.1 says to stop and ask if the tab isn't there. Questions (3) and (4) above now ask the retention and the drill's timing, ב applied in both. (11) Tests the review found missing, each mutation-checked (the guard removed alone fails a test): a plain file changed before encryption ("hash", the half-written file begun and gone), the written file changed or another manifest read back ("damaged", nothing left), `..` that only the words lead into the repo (`physicalPath` outside, `folderRefusal` "repo", nothing made), and a link moved into the repo between the two checks (refused, an empty folder only); and the sweep, the staging folder, the second listing, SIGHUP, `local`, `unrelated` and the upload count. (12) **Nothing kept the scripts on Node 20**: both test files scan their script as `check-site.test.ts` does (only `node:` imports, and `./backup-files.mjs` for the restore; no `@supabase/`, no `require(` or `import(`, none of Node 22's APIs). Docs: B8 steps 2 to 4 and the release section, README, this entry. Tests: `backup-files.test.ts` 60 (from 43), `restore-files.test.ts` 21 (from 17), `production-doc.test.ts` pins step 0, the reminder, the ref check, the synced folder, the retention and the second drill. Checked: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (Biome, `next typegen`, tsc, 5990 tests); on Node 20.11, both scripts imported, a round trip with a sweep, a stuck `rm`, the restore to a copy and to an unrelated project, the signals registered, and the command without a terminal. Not run here: against the local stack or any real project, a real window closed, `pnpm build`, e2e and pgTAP (CI). No migration, no new npm package; no SMS, push or email was sent.

## 2026-11-28 · chat · closing the pilot, PR 7: an install button on Android

- On chat/install-button from origin/main 5872aa20 (#288). The plan's PR 7 only: on Android, "התקנת האפליקציה" through Chrome's `beforeinstallprompt`, with words that say where the menu does it when there is no event, in PR 6's Today card and in `/notifications/settings`. No migration, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component, nothing from a later PR. No SMS, push or email; no customer named.
- **Installable**: `app/manifest.ts` has name, short name, `start_url`, `display: standalone`, 192 and 512 icons and no `prefer_related_applications`, web.dev's criteria today. `public/sw.js` has no fetch handler on purpose and is registered only once notifications are turned on; the criteria list no worker, but Chrome's own post said its automatic prompt still wanted a fetch handler for a while. Whether Chrome sends the event here is for a real Android to show; without it the menu's words show, the plan's fallback.
- **The capture**, `app/install-capture.ts`, run first in `<body>` by the root layout as an inline script made of the function's own source (`installCaptureScript(offered)`, `offered` being `pushConfig() !== null`), so it listens before any app code or React: keeps the event on `window`, with `preventDefault()` (no Chrome bar over the tab bar) only where `pushConfig()` is set, so the app can draw its button; elsewhere Chrome's bar is left to Chrome. Never calls `prompt()`; `appinstalled`, or the page opened from the home screen, keeps `conceptapp:app-installed` in localStorage (try/catch; on Android the app and Chrome share storage), and a new event clears it. Never on `/station`, whose own manifest installs the station. The app's only `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, with its `biome-ignore` reason. No policy has `script-src` today; one added later needs this script's hash or a nonce.
- **The rules**, `@domain/install`: `installStart` (nothing standalone, on an iPhone or iPad, off Android or in an app's web view; installed while open; the button once caught; nothing once seen installed; else the menu's words), `installBrowser`/`installMenu` (Chrome's menu, or the browser's for Samsung Internet, Edge, Firefox and the like), `installAnswer`, and `installChoice` (Chrome's `userChoice` through Zod).
- **The button**: `install-prompt.ts` (a store for `useSyncExternalStore`; `promptInstall` calls `prompt()` before anything is awaited, once per event) and `install-button.tsx` (`InstallButton`, live or a twin's, and `InstallView`): the words, then a secondary button with its spinner; after a press it says installed, or declined with the menu's words for later, the focus going to those words when it was left with nowhere (`focusOrSay`). In Today's card, last, after the card's own buttons, under a line, in every state but Safari's, whose iPhone steps stay as they are (`PushAskTodayCard`'s `install`), so never on the station, in a demo or viewing as someone. In the settings, a card of its own, "האפליקציה במסך הבית", beside the switch (push set up, no demo), not on the business's clock station (`currentOn`).
- Twins: Today's `?push=…&install=ready|prompting|installed|dismissed|menu` and the settings' `?install=`; `/design` has "כפתור ההתקנה באנדרואיד", each state in Hebrew and English. Words: `app.notifications.install.*` (menu: "בתפריט של Chrome (שלוש הנקודות): הוספה למסך הבית", or "בתפריט של הדפדפן"), `design.installApp*`.
- Tests. Unit: `packages/domain/install.test.ts` (12), `app/install-capture.test.ts` (15, each case by the function and by the layout's script run through `new Function`: caught and prevented, `appinstalled` and its mark, the mark cleared, standalone, the station left to Chrome, blocked storage, once only), `install-prompt.test.ts` (12, a fake page and event: no event, caught and never asked by itself, installed then nothing, standalone, iPhone and computer, the server, the press asking within itself, accepted, dismissed, one prompt per event, refused, none). e2e, with a fake event since headless Chromium sends none: `install.spec.ts` (caught as the page is parsed, left to Chrome on `/station`); `push-card.spec.ts` on the phone project (the menu's words; the button; a no, its words focused; nothing kept; a yes, `appinstalled`, then nothing on Today or in the settings; offered again, the settings install it; nothing on a computer); `design.spec.ts` (six clean pages, the twins' press); `screens.spec.ts` (none in a demo).
- Docs: ROADMAP (the notifications row, "מה לא נבדק"), `packages/domain/README.md`, this entry. Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (5827 tests). Not run here: Playwright, `pnpm build`, `next start`, `pnpm dev`, the screens by eye; so the inline script as Next's build compiles it is first checked by `install.spec.ts` in CI.
- Against the code at 5872aa20 (followed the code): (1) no `beforeinstallprompt` before, as the plan says; (2) the iPhone steps were already shared, so nothing is duplicated; (3) the plan doesn't name the station's own manifest (scope `/station`), so its pages are left out of the capture; (4) the settings had no station check, added for the button alone; (5) Chrome sends the event on computers too: kept to Android, and Samsung Internet gets the button; (6) the worker has no fetch handler.
- Defaults, to ask Orel: (1) Chrome's own bar is stopped on every page of the app where notifications to the phone are set up (after review; before it, everywhere); the offer is the app's, and Chrome's menu still installs. Alternative: let Chrome show its bar too. (2) Android only. Alternative: computers too. (3) Only where notifications are offered: no card on Today once they are on or after "לא עכשיו", so the settings alone. Alternative: a row on `/me`. (4) "(שלוש הנקודות)" added to the plan's words; the item's Hebrew name is for the real-phone round. (5) A "no" keeps nothing. (6) No fetch handler added. Alternative, if a real Android sends no event: one that only passes requests on.
- Next: CI runs the new e2e first; Orel's round on a real Android (does Chrome offer it, after how long, what its menu calls it); then PR 8. `docs/agent/LOG.md` is near Biome's 1 MiB limit (1,038,171 bytes before this entry): older entries should move to `log/` soon.
- After review (eight findings, five issues, all real once read in the code; each fixed the smallest way that holds, but (5)): (1) **After a no, installing from Chrome's menu on the same page still said it wasn't installed** (two findings). `answer ?? start` let a press's answer hide every later change. Now `installShown` (`@domain/install`): the answer shows while the offer still starts where the press left it (`over`), so `appinstalled` says installed and a new event brings the button back. Unit: one case; e2e (`push-card.spec`): a no, `appinstalled`, the installed words, then Chrome's offer again on the same page and its button. (2) **Chrome's bar was stopped where the app can never offer its button** (two findings). The root layout passes `pushConfig() !== null` to `installCaptureScript`: with no notifications to the phone, the event is kept and the bar left to Chrome. Unit: a case by the function and by the script; `install.spec` expects the bar stopped only with VAPID keys. Not done: the demo's address has push, so its bar is still stopped though a demo business never draws the button (CI's e2e builds with the door open, and that site may hold businesses that aren't demos). Default (1) now: stopped only where push is set up, signed-out pages and the console included. For Orel: also left to Chrome on the demo's address (`DEMO_DOOR` on)? (3) **Today's card drew the install button before its own answer** (two findings). It now comes last, after the card's buttons, under a line, its button across the card while theirs are, so a late event grows the card below them. Kept in every state but Safari's: the review's other option, only once on, is a screen choice the plan doesn't make. e2e (`design.spec`): the last button in the card, below "לא עכשיו". (4) **An Android tablet asking for the desktop site was a computer.** `installStart` takes a `Linux x86_64` user agent with touch (`maxTouchPoints > 1`) for Android, as `isAppleMobile` takes an iPad saying Mac; a touch Linux computer too, a Chromebook not. Unit: one case, and two checks for computers. (5) **An app installed but not marked here gets the menu's words** (not changed in code): `getInstalledRelatedApps` needs `related_applications` in the manifest, which touches installability, not yet seen on a real Android. Written in the ROADMAP's "מה לא נבדק" and on `installedBefore`. Checked: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (5831 tests; earlier runs timed out in unrelated tests under load, which passed alone and in the last run). Not run here: Playwright (the changed steps run first in CI), `pnpm build`, the screens by eye. No migration, no new npm package; no SMS, push or email was sent. ## 2026-11-28 · chat · closing the pilot, PR 8: write to whoever corrects your hours

- On chat/ask-fix from origin/main 73927418 (#286). The pilot-closing plan's PR 8 only (section 1, B, with its second-pass corrections): a worker who forgot to clock out sees, under the clock's refusal and beside "צריך תיקון" in their shifts, "כתיבה ל{name}", which opens their private chat (`/chat/with/<them>`) with someone who may correct their hours. It bridges the gap until the worker's own request for a correction (ROADMAP, "אחר כך"). Nothing is sent from the clock: the chat opens, empty or as it is, and the worker writes. One small migration, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component, no customer named.
- **Who** (`@domain/ask-fix`, pure, with its tests): those who may edit the clock in the business (`clock.edit`, as `private.holds_perm` answers it: an active owner always, anyone else by their role's rule, a named role's included, or a grant of their own), for the whole business: there is no department manager. `hoursFixers` never offers the person themself; offers the owners only when nobody else may; puts those who share a live department with the worker first, then the rest, each by name, the nameless last (`byNameThenId`, as the team's lists order people); marks who is written to in chat (chat open to the worker, and the other on the staff); and drops someone who can be neither written to nor named (chat off, no name) before choosing, so the owners still come when nobody else can be shown. `askFixOf` makes it one person to write to ("כתיבה ל{name}"; "כתיבה למי שמאשר שעות" for one with no name), a choice among several (the same words, opening a sheet of them, each with their departments, else their role), or, when chat reaches none of them, their names ("אפשר לבקש תיקון מ{name}.", or "מי שמאשר שעות בעסק: {names}." as one list).
- **The read**, `supabase/migrations/20261128000800_clock_editors.sql`: `public.clock_editors(business)`, security definer, stable, empty search path: the active members who hold `clock.edit`, each with their role and the business's name for them (`member_names`, else their profile's, null for none), ordered by id, to the business's own active members under their own session only (`private.is_member`): nothing for another business's member, someone who left, a view session, the secret key, an unknown business, or while the clock is off. Granted to `authenticated`, never `anon`. It writes nothing. `getClockEditors` in `@db/time-clock` reads it page by page (`readAllPages`, `order("user_id")`), checks the rows with Zod (a role the app knows, a name that may be missing) and answers none before the migration reaches a project. `database.types.ts` by hand, as the generator prints it.
- **The clock** (`app/(app)/clock`): `clockAccess` now says whether chat is open to the person (`chat`: on the staff, and chat's module opens for them, as `chatAccess` asks it). `askFixFor` reads `clock_editors` and the business's departments (`getDepartments`, under RLS), puts them through `hoursFixers` and `askFixOf`, and writes each person's chat address on the server (`HoursFixerLink.href`, `chatPaths.withPerson`, null for someone only named). Null for whoever edits the clock themself, while viewing as someone, and when nobody else may; a read that fails is logged by its code only and leaves it out, so the clock still opens and its refusal still says whom to ask in words. `currentClock` reads it with the rest (a refusal comes from a press); `currentClockHistory` only for a month with a shift that needs fixing. `ClockData.askFix` and `HistoryData.askFix` carry it (`ClockAskFix`); the `/design/shell` mocks pass none (their manager edits the clock; Noa's mock shifts need no fixing).
- **On screen**: `AskFix` (`ask-fix.tsx`, a client piece in `app/`, composing `Button`, `Sheet`, `PersonList` and `PersonRow` with app data, as `invite-share.tsx` does: no look of its own, so no `/design` entry). In `ClockPanel`, under the refusals that say to ask whoever approves hours (`FIX_FAILURES` in `@domain/time-clock-format`: `tooLong`, the shift open over 24 hours, the forgot-to-clock-out case, and `tooLate` and `times`), outside the alert, which says only why. In `ShiftList`, beside "צריך תיקון" on a shift that needs fixing, in the person's own shifts only (`askFix`, ignored with `onEdit`, whose rows are buttons): under the row's lines on phones and tablets, beside the badge in the desktop table. `HistoryView` passes it; `ClockView`'s recent shifts don't (see defaults). Touch targets 44 px (`Button` md), the icon beside words, `aria-haspopup="dialog"` on the choice, the sheet giving the focus back to its button.
- **The clock's download stays without Zod**: `tooling/boundaries.test.ts` holds the clock's browser files to the two Zod-free `@domain` files. `chatPaths` imports `@domain/activity`, which imports Zod, so the addresses come from the server and `ask-fix.tsx` imports only types besides `@ui`; `FIX_FAILURES` and `asksForFix` live in `time-clock-format.ts`, which imports nothing. `ask-fix.tsx` is now in the guarded list.
- **Words** (he and en, `app.clock.history.askFix.*`): `write`, `writeAny`, `pickTitle` ("למי לכתוב על התיקון", the picker's title), `pickHint`, `close`, `noName`, `named`, `namedSome`. No "!", every sentence with its full stop; the English twin has the same keys and placeholders.
- Tests. Unit: `packages/domain/ask-fix.test.ts` (15): the one person other than the owners; several, the worker's department first, each by name, with their departments; only live departments count; only owners, the owner, and several owners to pick from; never the worker themself (a worker who holds `clock.edit` of their own gets the owner; a sole owner asks nobody), whatever the case of the id; chat off names them, leaving out who has no name, and falls back to the owners when nobody else has a name; a nameless person kept in chat, after the named; someone off the staff named, not written to; each person once; nobody is nothing; a choice when only some are in chat; the three refusals, typed as `PunchFailure`, and no others. `app/(app)/clock/ask-fix.test.ts` (15, drawn on the server): the link to the chat by name, a nameless one, the choice's button with its sheet closed, one name and several with chat off, a person with no chat address named, English; each of the three refusals' Hebrew says "מי שמאשר שעות"; the words plain; the clock offering it after the alert for a shift open 26 hours, not within the day, not with nobody to ask; the shifts offering it in both shapes, not for a shift that needs no fixing, never in a manager's list. pgTAP: `supabase/tests/database/clock_editors.test.sql` (13): what a worker reads on the defaults (the owner, the managers by the business's name or none for one nobody named, a worker with a grant of their own; never the accountant, who left or a worker without it), the accountant the same, managers lowered, workers raised, another business's owner (nothing, and their own), who left, a view session, an unknown business, the secret key, the clock off, the grants, security definer and stable with an empty search path. E2E: `e2e/ask-fix.spec.ts`, a real business of its own (`ASK_FIX_BUSINESS` in `e2e/db.ts`, the shop's template, owner, a manager in the checkout, the worker in the storeroom clocked in 26 hours ago; numbers 050-000-0916 to 0919, ids `…0916` to `…0919` and `…00f3`, in no other spec or pgTAP file): the one manager by name on `/clock` under "המשמרת פתוחה יותר מ-24 שעות", not the owner, and on `/clock/history` (the month the shift began in) beside "צריך תיקון", then the press opening their chat, with axe and no sideways scroll, at 390, 820 and 1440; at 390, with a second manager in the worker's storeroom, the sheet with her first and the checkout's manager second, each with their department, then her chat; and at 390 with chat off, the name only, no link.
- Docs: `packages/domain/README.md` (`ask-fix.ts`), `packages/db/README.md` (`clock_editors`, `getClockEditors`), ROADMAP (the clock's row, and "בקשת תיקון שעות" under "אחר כך" says what bridges it), this entry.
- Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (Biome, `next typegen`, tsc, the unit tests; `hebrew-literals.test.ts` timed out once under load and passed alone and in the rerun). Not run here: Playwright (the spec is new, its first run is this PR's CI), pgTAP and `pnpm test:db` (no Docker here: `clock_editors.test.sql` and the migration first run in CI's database job, which also checks `database.types.ts` against `pnpm db:types`), `pnpm build`, `pnpm dev`, and the screens by eye at 390, 820 and 1440 (the spec photographs `/clock` in test-results/).

(2) No read gave members by permission to a member: `public.my_permissions` answers for oneself, `public.role_permissions` is the owners' to read (`20261115000800_roles_setter.sql:304-308`), `role_permission_matrix` is the secret key's, `private.members_holding` is private, and the chat's people list (`getChatDirectory`) has roles, no permissions, so `@domain/role-access` has nothing to compute from under a worker's session. (6) Chat's floor is view (`20261115001400_roles_chat.sql`): everyone on the staff writes, so "not reachable in chat" today is only someone off the staff, who never holds `clock.edit`; handled all the same. - After review (four findings, three issues, all real once read in the code; each fixed the smallest way that holds): (1) **pgTAP's "the secret key reads nothing" ran with the worker still signed in** (two findings, the same line). `pg_temp.sign_in` sets `request.jwt.claims` until the rollback, and `reset role` leaves them, so under `service_role` `auth.uid()` was still p(3), an active worker of A with its clock on: `clock_editors(A)` gave p1, p2, p4 and p8, and the `is_empty` would have failed CI's database job every time, without testing a call with nobody signed in. `clock_editors.test.sql` now has `pg_temp.sign_out()`, as `employee_files.test.sql` does, and calls it before `set local role service_role`. Still 13 assertions; `$$` counted, 40 before and 42 after (the two of `sign_out`). Production was never affected: a real secret-key request carries no `sub`. (2) **A clock-out forgotten for less than 24 hours had no button.** The clock takes a clock-out up to 24 hours after the clock-in, so a worker in at 15:00 who presses at 08:00 the next morning (or a few minutes before their next daily shift) is recorded with a long shift, never "צריך תיקון", and saw only "יצאת ב-…" and "ביטול היציאה". Taken on the recommendation, as a default for Orel (12, below): `LONG_ENTRY_MINUTES` (14 hours) and `longShift` in `@domain/time-clock-format` (no Zod, so the clock's browser files may take it), and `LIMITS.entry` in `@domain/payroll-flags` is now that constant, so the clock and payroll's `longEntry` flag can't drift apart; the same for every kind of business, no new setting. In `ClockPanel`, under the "יצאת ב-…" card (its two minutes), a shift that counted over 14 hours gets `app.clock.history.askFix.long` ("המשמרת נספרה יותר מ-14 שעות. אם שכחת לצאת בזמן, מי שמאשר שעות יכול לתקן אותה.", en "The shift counted more than 14 hours. If you forgot to clock out on time, whoever approves hours can fix it.", the hours from the constant inside `<ltr>`) and `AskFix`. In `ShiftList`, in the person's own shifts only, a closed shift that counted over 14 hours gets the same line and `AskFix`: under its lines on phones and tablets, under its hours in the desktop table; no "צריך תיקון" badge, since it was recorded. `currentClockHistory` reads whom to ask for a month with a shift that needs fixing or one that long. The list goes by `record.minutes`, the hours it shows (rounded by the business's setting, unpaid breaks off), so at 14 hours it can differ from the engine by the rounding step. Nothing for whoever edits the clock, or with nobody to ask, as before. (3) **Today said "במשמרת מ-16:00" for one's own shift open over 24 hours** (low; from before this PR). `currentClockLine` now returns `{ kind: "ownOverdue" }` when the person's own open shift is `entryOverdue` (after the team's counts for whoever edits the clock, which already count it), and Today's clock row says `app.clock.todayLine.ownOverdue` ("צריך תיקון: המשמרת פתוחה יותר מ-24 שעות", en "Needs fixing: the shift has been open over 24 hours"), still leading to `/clock`, where the refusal and whom to ask are. No migration. Tests: `packages/domain/ask-fix.test.ts` (2 new: over 14 hours and closed is long, `LONG_ENTRY_MINUTES` is `LIMITS.entry`; 14 hours to the minute, 8 hours or one still open are not); `app/(app)/clock/ask-fix.test.ts` (7 new: the clock offering whom to ask under "יצאת ב-…" after 17 hours with the line and no alert, not after 7 h 42 or exactly 14 hours, not with nobody to ask; the shifts offering it beside a 17-hour shift in both shapes with its line and no badge, not with nobody to ask, never in a manager's list; the long line's words, and Today's line leading with "צריך תיקון" and saying 24 in he and en). Today itself is an async server view no unit test draws; tsc checks its key. No e2e for the long line: the spec's worker is clocked in 26 hours ago and never out, which is Today's `ownOverdue`, in the e2e since the second review (below). Docs: `packages/domain/README.md` (`longShift`), ROADMAP (the clock's row). New defaults, to ask Orel: (10) the words of the long line and of Today's. (11) Today's row for one's own shift open over a day leads with "צריך תיקון", in the row's usual colour. ## 2026-11-28 · chat · closing the pilot, PR 6: a notifications card on Today for everyone

- On chat/push-card from origin/main bb878bc0 (#285). The pilot-closing plan's PR 6 only (section 1, B): workers, managers, owners and the accountant are asked on Today to turn on notifications on the phone until this device has them or they say "לא עכשיו", and an iPhone's Safari no longer uses up the one offer. No migration, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component, nothing from PR 7 (the Android install button) or any later PR. Never a push to anyone: nothing here subscribes on its own, and no test does. No customer is named anywhere.
- **The rules, pure**, `@domain/push`: `pushAskStart` (how the card starts in this browser: nothing where push can't come, is blocked or is on already; the steps to the home screen in an iPhone's or iPad's Safari, unless they were put away in this browser or the person's home-screen app there has notifications already; else the way to turn them on), `installedHere` (an iPhone or iPad label among the person's devices; never a computer; an iPad asking for the desktop site is an iPad by `browserDeviceLabel`, after the check), `pushAskMark(place, state, event)` (what each event keeps: for the person, for this browser, or nothing) and `deviceKinds` (Zod, the labels read back leniently, each once). The steps are never counted for the person, at Today or at a moment. A moment's card is kept for the person once it is pressed, as before, and only seen, in this browser alone (after the check). Today's is kept only by "לא עכשיו" while it still offers something: seen and left, it comes again; after a press that turned them on, or found them blocked, "סגירה" keeps nothing.
- **Per person, and the steps per browser** (the task left the choice): "לא עכשיו" keeps `push_asked_at` (`public.see_push_ask`), the column the moments' card already writes, one row per person across their businesses. So after it neither card comes on any of their devices, which is the "without asking twice", and it needs no migration (this PR may have none, and a per-device record in the database would). The steps alone are kept in the browser (localStorage `conceptapp:push-install-put-away`, inside try/catch): a per-person mark there is the bug itself, and an iPhone keeps Safari's storage apart from the home-screen app's, so the app, once installed, still offers the switch. A private window or blocked storage shows the steps again next time.
- **The offer**, `currentPushAsk` (`push-ask-offer.ts`): also never in a demo business by its slug (`isDemoBusiness`), besides `pushAllowed`, which is per person; and with `devices`, the kinds of the person's own devices, read under their own session (`ownDeviceKinds`, `@db/push/devices`, the `label` column the browser may read, "people read their own devices"; none when it can't be read), so Safari on an iPhone whose home-screen app has notifications, opened from a link (known issue 21), shows no steps. Today's "לא עכשיו" keeps what `seePushAskAction` keeps through a route of its own, `/home/push-ask` (after the second review: it was a server action, `putAwayPushAskAction`), without drawing the screen again, since the write is held for its undo and can go after Today was left or as the page closes.
- **The card**, `app/(app)/notifications/push-ask-today.tsx`: `PushAskToday` on `TodayView` (under the hero, in its column; with none, after the rest) and on `AccountantHome` (under the month to pay; with the clock off, after the rest), never on the business's clock station, which isn't the person's device (placed so after review: it was above the hero). Quiet (`flat`, its button secondary: the screen's accent and main action stay where they were). By its own width (after review): narrow (a phone, the hero's column, the gallery), the words, then the buttons one under the other across the card; wide, the words at the start, the buttons at the end, `md:max-w-2xl`. Off: "רוצה לקבל התראות בטלפון?" (במכשיר on anything not a phone, by what the browser is), the existing body, "הפעלת התראות בטלפון" and "לא עכשיו"; the press turns them on there and then, as the push switch does (`turnOnPush`), and says what came of it: on (with the settings' link), blocked or failed. Safari on an iPhone: "התראות באייפון", its body and the three steps (`IosInstallSteps`, now shared with the settings' card), and "לא עכשיו". "לא עכשיו" goes as Today's other cards go: an `UndoRow` in its place with the focus, the write held (`useHeldWrite`), "ביטול" brings it back with the focus on "לא עכשיו", and once the row goes the focus goes to the next section's title; Today is drawn again once the person's mark is kept, while it is on screen. It is never brought into view and doesn't say itself as it comes (Today is being read then); the answer to each press is said (`announce`), the same words again too (after the second review). A press whose button went keeps the keyboard in the card.
- **The moments' card**, `push-ask.tsx`: the same start (`usePushAskState`, `push-ask-state.ts`, shared) and `pushAskMark("moment", …)`: pressed, kept for the person as before, except the steps, kept in this browser only; only seen, kept in this browser (after the check). (5) The menu's departments keep "מטבח או בר", since only waiter mode shows them.
- After review (three findings, all real once read in the code; each fixed the smallest way that holds): (1) **The restaurant-word guard took common words for a bar or a dish.** `RESTAURANT_ONLY` let any of "ושכלמבה" come before the short words, so "כבר" (already, all over he.json), "שכבר", "ממנה" (from her) and "לשבר" matched it, and an example that gained "אם כבר" would have failed for nothing. Now a long word ("מטבח", "קופה", "שולחן" and the like) still takes any letters before it, and a short one ("בר", "שף", "מנה", "מנות") only after "ה" ("הבר", "שהבר", "מהבר") or after "ו", "ב" and "ל" alone ("לבר", "ובמנה"), and never "למנות" (to count). Over every word in he.json it matches what it matched before, less exactly those four. "בריסטה" joins the Hebrew list, as "barista" is in the English. The self-check finds "לבר", "שהבר", "מהבר", "השף", "המנות", "במנה" and "בריסטה", and, in a case of its own, not "כבר", "וכבר", "שכבר", "כשכבר", "ממנה", "שבר", "לשבר", "שמנה" or "למנות". (2) **The line above the code still said a code went to a number only invited.** `auth.signIn.codeIntro` was "אם המספר <ltr>{phone}</ltr> נוסף לצוות של עסק, שלחנו אליו קוד בן 6 ספרות.", but an invite makes no account, only joining does (`ensurePhoneAccount`, from `join/actions.ts`), and sign-in sends a code only to a number with one, so for an invited number that never joined nothing went and the line said the opposite of "לא הגיע קוד?" under it. Now "אם כבר הצטרפת לעסק עם המספר <ltr>{phone}</ltr>, שלחנו אליו קוד בן 6 ספרות." (en "If you've already joined a business with <ltr>{phone}</ltr>, we sent it a 6-digit code."): the same for every number, its `<ltr>{phone}</ltr>` kept for `sign-in-form.test.ts` and `e2e/code-slots.spec.ts`, which read it by key (the form test's case is now "has joined a business"). New case: "כבר הצטרפת לעסק" and no "נוסף", "already joined" and no "added". Left as it is, since the review's check found it no problem: `auth.enter.phoneIntro`, shown only on /enter while the demo door is on, which introduces the phone button and is not false. (3) **"עוד לא שייכים לעסק" sent someone whose invite was still open to ask for one.** Joining makes the account as its code is sent, so someone who pressed "שליחת קוד" on the link and never typed the code can sign in later and land on `NoBusinessScreen`, which looks for no open invite. Its line was "החשבון פעיל, אבל אף עסק עוד לא הוסיף אותו. מי שמנהל את העסק יכול לשלוח הזמנה למספר הזה.", though their invite was open and the link still joins, signed in or not (`/join/[token]` reads only the invite). Now "החשבון פעיל, אבל עוד לא הצטרפת לאף עסק. אם קיבלת קישור אישי בהודעה, פותחים אותו ומצטרפים. אין קישור, או שעברו 14 יום? מבקשים קישור חדש ממי שמנהל את העסק." (en "Your account works, but you haven't joined a business yet. If you got a personal link in a message, open it to join. No link, or more than 14 days have passed? Ask whoever runs your business for a new one."). A business can't reword it (`app.noBusiness.*` is not editable, `@domain/wording`), so he/en is the only place; `team-changes.spec.ts` reads it by key, and the comment on `no-business.tsx` says why the link comes first. New case: "עוד לא הצטרפת", the personal link, `INVITE_DAYS` days and "ממי שמנהל את העסק", and their twins in en. `worker-words.test.ts` now has 14 cases; the two new word cases fail against the words before. Checked: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (Biome, `next typegen`, tsc, 5709 tests; a first run timed out once in `hebrew-literals.test.ts` under load, which passed alone and in the rerun). Not run here: `pnpm build`, Playwright, the screens by eye. No migration, no new npm package; no SMS, push or email was sent.
- After the check (two findings, both real once read in the code; words in this LOG and one comment, no he.json change): (1) **The LOG said every other restaurant-only word was waiter mode's.** Two are the time clock's own: `app.clock.station.lock.full` (he.json:3129) and `.failure.tooManyStations` (:3131), drawn on the clock's lock (`lock-station.tsx`'s `tc("full")`) for any business with clock stations once it has `MAX_STATIONS` (10) locked devices, both "עמדות שעון, מסכי הכנה וקופות", though a business without taking orders has no prep screen or till. Only owners and managers read them, and they were so before this PR, so they stay, named in the "Left" bullet for a later PR to say only "עמדות שעון" while taking orders is off. A grep of he.json for the restaurant's words outside `app.orders.*`, `modules.order_taking.*` and `design.*` found one more group the sentence missed, the reservations module's (`modules.reservations.*`, `shell.reservations.*`, off in every template), and waiter mode's lines outside those keys (the activity log's menu and stations, the roles' hint, the orders' notifications, which `groupsFor` gives only with `order_taking`, the console's menu, and the clock's `failure.otherStation`, returned only for a device that is the business's prep screen or till); the "Left" bullet now names them; "קופה" in `app.pay.details.*` is a pension fund's, not a till. (2) **The departments view's comment named reception and the warehouse as the departments that prepare orders.** It now gives them as the groups lists go to and, while taking orders is on, the kitchen as one that prepares what is ordered, as `departments.subtitleMenu` says ("כמו מטבח או בר") and `worker-words.test.ts` pins. Tests: none changed, since neither the LOG nor a comment is read by a test and no word changed. Checked: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (Biome, `next typegen`, tsc, 5709 tests). Not run here: `pnpm build`, Playwright, pgTAP.
- After the second review (one finding, real once read in the code; tests only, no word changed): **the invite-days guard held every migration ever written to `INVITE_DAYS`.** It read every `expires_at … now() + interval 'N days'` in every migration (21 of them, from `20261013000100_tenancy.sql` to `20261128000300_people_import.sql`), so a later migration that made invites 7 days long, with `INVITE_DAYS` 7, would have failed on the old 14s for good, since an applied migration can't be edited; and it took any expiry counted in days, on any table, for an invite's. Now `inviteDaysInForce` reads only what runs once every migration has: the default of `public.invites.expires_at` as the newest `create table` or `alter table public.invites` that sets it has it, and each expiry an `update` or `insert` of `public.invites` writes in a function as it runs, found by `functionsInForce`, new in `tooling/role-checks.ts` beside the guard's own versions (a later version replaces one, a drop ends it, a rename takes it along; each version now keeps its text). Today that is the default and five functions (`issue_invite_link`, `team_invite_link`, `admin_invite`, `team_invite`, `private.import_invites`), all 14. Another table's expiry and a copy of another (`excluded.expires_at`) count for none, and an invite's expiry written another way (`interval '2 weeks'`, `make_interval`, an insert's own value) counts as no number, so it fails rather than goes unseen. Three new cases on made-up migrations: a later migration's 7 days replaces the 14 and a dropped function's goes; a share link's 30 and 60 days and a copied expiry count for none; the other ways are seen. `worker-words.test.ts` now has 17 cases. Checked: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (Biome, `next typegen`, tsc, 5712 tests; a first run timed out in `captcha.test.ts`, `demo-door.test.ts` and `hebrew-literals.test.ts` under load, which passed alone and in the rerun). Not run here: `pnpm build`, Playwright, pgTAP.

## 2026-11-28 · chat · closing the pilot, PR 4: every invite goes out on WhatsApp, with a sentence

- On chat/invite-whatsapp from origin/main ed2f17e5 (#282). The pilot-closing plan's PR 4 only (section 1, B): the owner's first link from the console, a new hire's link and a renewed 14-day link arrive as a WhatsApp message to the person's own number, with a sentence that says what it is. Until now only "עוד לא קיבלו קישור" had WhatsApp (`send-links.tsx`, `whatsappToHref`); the invite form, "קישור חדש" and the console's link showed the bare link to copy. The app still sends nothing: wa.me opens WhatsApp with the message written, and the person sending presses send there (Orel, 25.11). No migration, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component, no customer named.
- **The shared piece**, `app/invite-share.tsx`: `InviteShare` composes `Button` and `CopyLink` with app data, as `app/copy-link.tsx` does, so it stays in `app/` (it has no look of its own: no `/design` entry). "שליחה בוואטסאפ" first, a real link opened by the press itself (`target="_blank"`, `rel="noopener noreferrer"`), named "שליחה בוואטסאפ ל{name}, עם הקישור", clear of the tab bar when the focus is brought to it (`clearOfTabBar`, moved here from `send-links.tsx`); "נפתח בוואטסאפ" once pressed (kept by the screen where every shape says it: "עוד לא קיבלו קישור" in `PendingLinks`, and "קישור חדש" in `InviteLinks`, after the check; or by the piece itself, where only one shape has the link: the invite form, drawn once, and the console's link, which each shape makes for itself); then `CopyLink` as the second way. `main` puts WhatsApp in the accent, large: the invite form's one main action (the copy was, before). `InviteShareSetup` is `{ business, toNumber }`; `InviteWords` is `team` or `owner`. The text is the sentence and the link on its own line (`shareText`), and names nobody: no person, no number.
- **The four screens**: (1) the invite form's ready card (`invite-form.tsx`), on `/team/invite` and on the console's "הוספת אדם" (`add-person-view.tsx`, the same form), with `share` and, in the console, `ownerWords`; `InviteState.done` now carries the invite's base `role` (both actions and the twin's stand-in), so an owner added from the console gets the owner's sentence. (2) "קישור חדש" (`resend-link.tsx`), on `/team` and on the import's done screen for those whose link ran out (`DoneExpired`, which takes the send list's `share`), with the invite's `phone`. (3) The console's link on a business's page (`invite-link.tsx`, from `business-view.tsx`), the owner's sentence for an owner's invite, the team's for anyone else. (4) "עוד לא קיבלו קישור" (`SendLinkRow`), its button and message moved into the shared piece, behaviour unchanged. A new link on (2) and (3) now puts the focus on "שליחה בוואטסאפ", as (4) already did (it was on the copy button); with the focus put elsewhere meanwhile it stays there and the announcer says the link is ready, as before.
- **To the number, never on a twin**: `inviteWhatsappHref` in `@domain/share` opens a chat to the number the link was made for (`whatsappToHref`, kept as it was), or, with none to trust, asks whom (`whatsappHref`). Each real page says `toNumber` (`/team`, `/team/invite`, `/team/import`, the console's business page and "הוספת אדם": `whatsappToNumber`, false by default); every `/design` twin leaves it off, so its made-up numbers are never in a link, as `send-links.tsx` already did. A number that isn't E.164, or none (a console row's type allows it), asks whom too rather than breaking the screen. The number goes into no URL but that wa.me link, which the person opens themselves. Nothing here reaches a demo business: the database makes no link for one (`private.closed_to_invites` in `team_invite`, `team_invite_link`, `admin_invite` and `issue_invite_link`; `/team/invite` is not found there), and only the local seed's demo restaurant takes invites, for the e2e.
- **The words** (he and en): `app.team.pending.message` is now "{business} מזמינים אותך לצוות באפליקציה ConceptApp. הקישור אישי ובתוקף 14 יום. פותחים אותו מהטלפון הזה ומקבלים קוד ב-SMS." (it ended "עם המספר הזה:", which read the link as the number, and never said a code comes by SMS), and the console's own `admin.setup.invite.ownerMessage`: "הקישור האישי שלך לניהול {business} באפליקציה ConceptApp. פותחים אותו מהטלפון הזה ומקבלים קוד ב-SMS." The words for WhatsApp itself stay `app.team.pending.whatsapp`, `whatsappFor` and `opened`, read by every screen (the first two reworded after review, point 4).
- **The business as a name** (`isolateName` in `@domain/bidi`, FSI … PDI): the new sentence starts with the business's name, and WhatsApp takes a message's direction from its first letter outside any isolate, so a business named in Latin letters ("Bakery 12 מזמינים אותך…") would have laid the whole Hebrew message out left to right. Held so, the name keeps its own direction and the sentence keeps its. Any isolate or pop in the name is dropped first. A platform that ignores isolates shows it as it would have without them.

## 2026-11-28 · chat · closing the pilot, PR 1b: the other screens survive a release

- On chat/stale-build-rest from origin/main a0158c82 (#280). The pilot-closing plan's PR 1b only (section 1, A, PR 1, second part), building on PR 1a (#278): the same helper in the catch paths people press most, the chat composer's line and button, and waiter mode's writes, which closes known issue 37. No migration, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component, nothing from a later PR. No customer is named anywhere.
- **The note moved into the frame**, `app/release-note.tsx` (new): `ReleaseNote` wraps the screen in the app's frame (`app/(app)/layout.tsx`, around `{children}` only, so nothing else moves) and in the design shell (`app/design/shell/layout.tsx`, Shiran). It hands the presses under it the signed-in person (`useSignedInId()`, React context; null outside a frame, as in the /design gallery), and on the page loaded again it takes the note (`takeUpdatedNote`) and says "האפליקציה התעדכנה, והלחיצה לא נרשמה. לוחצים שוב." once, in its own `StatusToast`, whichever screen it is: drawn only on such a page, its live region first and its words a drawing later, so screen readers hear them, and so no other page has a second status region beside the screen's own (`schedule-build.spec.ts` looks for "the" status). The words are given once, so the frame drawn again by a screen's `refresh()` doesn't say them again. The toast goes at the person's next press (a click caught on the document), so it never lies over the screen's own toast in the same place, which says what that press did; else after its 4 seconds, as before. The clock panel no longer takes the note nor draws it; `ClockData.userId`, added by PR 1a only for this, is gone (`clock-types.ts`, `clock.ts`, the twin's `data.ts`). Every press now takes its person from the frame, so the note left and the frame that says it are the same person by construction (the twins' screens carry people of their own: the list twin is Shiran's or Omer's, the clock's was "demo").
- **The helper**, `app/stale-build.ts`: `reloadForRelease(error, person, keep?)` takes `string | null`; no person (or an empty one) loads nothing and leaves no mark, so a press outside a frame says its failure as before. New, for the chat: `reloadKeepingWords(person, words, announce)` keeps the field's words, and whether they were an announcement, in sessionStorage (`stale-build-kept`, the one record kept, JSON read back through Zod: time, person, the page's path, the screen's name, the words) and loads the page; pressed only, so no minute's guard and no note; nothing written or nobody known keeps nothing, and storage that refuses loads the page all the same. `takeKeptWords(person)` gives them back once to that person at that path within 5 minutes and lets them go; someone else's, too old or unread go saying nothing, another chat's stay for it. `forgetOthersKept(person)` runs as the frame opens, so what was kept for one person (a reload that went to the door) never waits for whoever signs in next. The header comment now says what PR 1a left for here (below).
- **The presses**, each `if (reloadForRelease(error, signedIn)) await untilReloaded();` with `const signedIn = useSignedInId();`, then the press's own failure: a week's availability sent (`availability-form.tsx`), a list item's change, its tick, count, reading, note or comment (`list-screen.tsx`, `saveNow`), and a swap's step, give away, withdraw or take (`swap-button.tsx`, on the card and in the sheet, which stays held while the page goes). The availability's and the swap's actions threw into `useActionState`, so any thrown error went to the error screen: only the release's error is caught there, and a page that stays says the form's own "לא הצלחנו לשמור. כדאי לנסות שוב בעוד רגע." / the step's "משהו השתבש. כדאי לנסות שוב." with the week as written still on it; any other thrown error goes to the error screen as before. The list's catch already said "failed" for anything; the change stays shown while the page goes, and is put back with the list's own words should it stay. None sends its press again.
- **Waiter mode** (known issue 37): its twelve writes ("הוגש", "מוכן", החזרה, "להוציא", a line's cancel and "על חשבון הבית", the order's cancel, a move, a close, its "ביטול", a reopen, the label) are all bound in `app/(app)/orders/layout.tsx` and handed to every screen by `OrdersModeProvider`, so one wrapper covers them: `reloadOnRelease(actions, signedIn)` (`app/(app)/orders/release-writes.ts`, new) loads the page again for a release and waits, then throws as before, so each screen's own `.catch(() => null)` says its failure should the page stay; any other failure goes as it came, at once. It sits under `inTurn`, so a close or a move still waits for every write before it. The send is the outbox's route handler, which a release leaves as it was.
- **The chat**, `conversation-screen.tsx`: a text message whose send meets a page from the release before never went, and a retry from that page never would, so it leaves the conversation, its words go back to the field (before anything written since, on a line of their own), and the line over the field says "האפליקציה התעדכנה, וההודעה לא נשלחה. טוענים מחדש, וההודעה מחכה בשדה." with a "טעינה מחדש" button (`app.updated.chat`, `app.updated.reload`, he and en; the Composer's `notice` takes a node, its live line says it). It stays until the page goes. Pressed, it keeps what is in the field and loads the page, which puts it back in the field for the person to send. The page never loads by itself. A picture or a PDF that meets one says it didn't go, with its retry, as before.
- **A press the new release still knows** (what #278's second review left for this PR): checked in the installed Next.js 16.4.6, `server-action-reducer.js`: the action runs on the new release and is recorded or refused; its own answer (`a`) is handed to the press, which says it, since the build-id check only drops the page it carries; then, when the action reads the page again (`refresh()`) or redirects on that path, the router reads it, that answer's build id differs (`fetch-server-response.js:163`), and the page loads whole a moment later, with what the press said; when it does neither, the old page stays and says the answer (corrected after review, below). PR 1a's comment said the answer was dropped; corrected. **Decided: left so, and written down** (`app/stale-build.ts`'s header, ROADMAP known issue 111, "מה לא נבדק"): a page loaded whole shows what came of the press (the shift running, the availability sent, the request made) and says nothing of it; a refusal is said again by the next press, made on the new page. A press can't tell which build answered it, nor that the page is about to load whole, so carrying its words across would mean every screen keeping what it says for a load that may never come, and its "ביטול" can't come along. The chat's send doesn't read the page again, so it leaves the old page as it was. Nothing retries a press.
- **The stale tab's list**, `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts` (7): `app/stale-build.ts` has two whole-page loads now, the press's and the chat's pressed one, with the reason; `reloadForRelease(` in five files: the clock panel's two, the availability form, the list screen, the swap button and waiter mode's wrapper (one call for its twelve writes), and nowhere else; each followed by `await untilReloaded();`; each component takes `const signedIn = useSignedInId();`, and the wrapper's one caller, the orders provider, hands it that; `takeUpdatedNote(` only in the frame; `<ReleaseNote userId=` in both frames; `reloadKeepingWords(` only in the chat's `onClick`, `takeKeptWords(` only in the chat, and no `reloadForRelease(` there.
- Tests. Unit: `app/stale-build.test.ts` (35, 13 new: no person loads nothing and keeps no guard; the chat's words kept with time, person, path and words, no note and no guard; given back once, just within 5 minutes, not to someone else (and gone), not to another chat (and kept for it), not too old, not unread or from after now (each gone); nothing written or nobody known keeps nothing and still loads, and takes an earlier keeping away; storage refusing still loads; storage unreadable or that can't let go gives nothing; the frame lets anyone else's go and keeps the person's own; the chat's words, he and en). `app/(app)/orders/release-writes.test.ts` (5: answers and arguments unchanged, sent once; any other failure at once with nothing loaded; a release's loads again for the person, waits 15 s, then throws, never sent again; within the minute at once; nobody, nothing loaded). `app/release-note.test.ts` (3, drawn on the server: the presses under it get its person, none outside it; the screen alone and unwrapped, no status region of the frame's). `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts` above. - After review (eleven findings, seven issues, all real once read in the code; each fixed the smallest way that holds): (1) **An announcement put back went out as an ordinary message** (three findings). `send()` turns "הודעה מההנהלה" off as a message goes, and `stayedFromRelease` put back only the words. It now turns the switch back on when the message was an announcement and the person may still make one. `reloadKeepingWords(person, words, announce)` keeps the switch with the words (the button passes `announcing`), and the chat loaded turns it on again with them when the person may still announce there (the restore effect moved below `canAnnounce` and `writable`). Unit: kept and given back, with it and without. (2) **Two messages that met the release came back in reverse order** (three findings). The actions run one after another, so the second came back second and went before the first (`${body}\n${now}`). New `app/(app)/chat/words-back.ts`, `wordsBack(field, given, item)`: the words given back so far stay in a ref, in the order they were written (`item.at`), and the field is those words, then what was written since the first came back. A field emptied meanwhile starts again with the new one; words given back and changed meanwhile take the new one after them. The field is read from a ref (`textNow`), not inside a state updater, which may run twice. `words-back.test.ts` (7). (3) **A second send after the line appeared said nothing** (one finding). While the line is up, `send()` sends no text message and leaves the words in the field, and the screen's own line says `app.updated.chat` again (its keyed span is heard at each press; the Composer's line doesn't change, so it isn't). A picture or a PDF still goes, and says it didn't go, as before. (4) **The record of a press the new release still knows was wrong** (two findings). Checked again in Next.js 16.4.6's `server-action-reducer.js`: with no redirect, no revalidation, and its page dropped by the build-id check, the reducer returns the state as it was, so nothing loads. Only a press whose action reads the page again or redirects on that path loads whole: the clock's, recorded or refused; the availability sent and a swap's step made, on success only; and a list refusal that moves on (`router.refresh()`). A list item's change (`refresh: false`), waiter mode's writes (none reads the page again), and the availability's and the swap's refusals (they answer before `refresh()`) keep the old page and say their answer. Corrected in `app/stale-build.ts`'s header, ROADMAP 111 and the bullet above. Default (2) stands, on this narrower ground. (5) **Availability: the week as marked was lost on the reload, and the note said to press again** (one finding, medium). A worker who took Thursday off and pressed Send on an old page got the old week back and, pressing again, "נשמר" for the week the server already had. Now the press keeps what the form sent (week, hours, note, read back through Zod) for the page loaded: `reloadForRelease(error, person, keep)`, with `keep` as `{ of, value, words }`. The form loaded takes it with `takeKept(person, "availability", schema)` (the same person and address, within 5 minutes, once), puts the days and the note back, marks the form changed, and sends nothing. The frame says the form's own words (`app.updated.availability`, "האפליקציה התעדכנה, והזמינות לא נשלחה. מה שסימנתם נשאר כאן: לוחצים שוב על שליחת הזמינות."). The note carries its words as `<time> <person> <words>`. A note with none, as PR 1a's clock leaves it on the release before, says the press's words, and so does one with words this release doesn't have. E2e: Monday marked before Send is still on the form after the reload, nothing saved, the availability's words said, and it is still there after the second press's failure. (6) **Lists: typed values were lost on the reload, the next field's typing too, and the words didn't fit** (one finding). The same `keep`: `typedToKeep` keeps each number's and note's field, an order's count and a comment, by item, where they differ from the item as saved here (the one being saved as it was before), so nobody else's value is put back over theirs. It takes the next field's typing and an open comment sheet too. A tick, its count, or a minus keeps nothing and says the press's words. The list loaded puts them back in their fields, unsaved (a comment in its sheet, opened again), and a field saves as it is left, as always. Words: `app.updated.typed`, "האפליקציה התעדכנה, ומה שכתבתם לא נשמר. הוא חזר למקום שבו כתבתם, ואפשר לשמור אותו שוב.". Waiter mode's takeaway name the same way: `reloadOnRelease(actions, signedIn, { setLabel: keepLabel })` keeps what a write's arguments carry, and the order's screen puts the name back in its field. E2e on the list twin: a note typed and left is back in its field after the reload, nothing is sent again, and the typed words are said. (7) **The chat's line was in no browser test** (one finding). A new phone-only test in `e2e/chat.spec.ts` (the chat specs write the shared demo, so they run once, with `clearDemoChat` around them) rewrites the action id of its own send only, so the chat's reads go as they are. The words come back to the field with no bubble, the line and its button show, the line is said, the page stays, and axe is clean with no overflow. A second send sends nothing and the screen's line says it again. "טעינה מחדש" loads the same address once, the words in the field, nothing sent. Not checked: the line at 820 and 1440 by eye (the twin sends nothing, and the real chat runs on the phone only). The helper: one kept record (`stale-build-kept`, `{at, person, where, of, value}` through Zod) for the chat's field and the screens' writing, the newest in place of any before it. `takeKept` gives it once to that person's screen at that address, in time; someone else's, too old or unread goes, and another screen's or another address's stays for it. `forgetOthersKept` runs in the frame, and a page that stays (`untilReloaded`) lets go of what its own press kept, matched by the note's time, never of something kept before it. Storage that can't keep the note, the guard and the record keeps none of them, and the page stays as before. `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts`: the three presses that keep and under which name, the three screens that take back, the wrapper's `keepLabel`, the chat's button with `announcing`. Unit: `app/stale-build.test.ts` (8 new: the record with the note's words, given back once by schema, another screen's or address's kept, someone else's, too old or unread gone, nothing kept within the minute or halfway, a page that stayed letting go of its own only, the note's words old and new, the two new sentences in he and en) and `release-writes.test.ts` (2 new). New defaults, to ask Orel: (8) A screen with something written on it reloads by itself, the writing comes back unsent or unsaved, and its own words say so (the review's option א). The alternative is the chat's way: no reload by itself, a line with "טעינה מחדש", and the writing back after the press. (9) Typed list values come back unsaved, and save as their field is left, as always. The alternative is to save them by themselves after the reload, at the reload's time. (10) The words, he and en, as above. Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (Biome, `next typegen`, tsc, 5636 tests). Not run here: `pnpm build`, Playwright (the changed `e2e/release.spec.ts` and the new chat test run first in CI), and the screens by eye. (2) **A list fix that met a release came back out of fix mode** (medium): the finished list loaded showed the unsaved correction as its answer, a read-only field left sent a refused write, and a comment was dropped. The kept record now carries `fix`, and `typedBack` (`app/(app)/lists/typed-back.ts`, new, with `KeptTyped` and `needsMinus` moved there from the screen, no React) decides what goes back: typed in a fix, a list the person may still fix (`can.fix`) opens being fixed again and takes it; otherwise only a list still being filled (`can.fill`) does. A list neither filled nor fixed takes nothing, so nothing unsaved reads as its answer and no read-only field has a draft to send. Only its own items take it, each as its kind does. (3) **The frame's words were fixed at the press** (low). The frame now says a screen's words only once that screen says it put the writing back: `usePutBack()` (`app/release-note.tsx`, in the frame's context beside `useSignedInId()`), called by each screen that takes back (the availability, the list, the order's takeaway name) as it opens, with whether it did; the first answer counts. `wordsOnceBack(noted, back)` (`app/stale-build.ts`): the press's words at once for a note in them; the screen's when it put back; the press's when it didn't (another week, a value its schema doesn't read, a list neither filled nor fixed, another order's name), or when no screen said within `PUT_BACK_WAIT_MS` (5 s: a write whose screen was left, a screen that failed); nothing when the person pressed meanwhile, as that press's screen says what came of it. A list change that meets the release after its screen was left (a count saved on the way out) keeps nothing (`onScreen`), so its note is the press's at once. (en "The app was updated, and your press wasn't recorded: press again. What you typed wasn't saved either, and it's back where you typed it."). The after-review bullet's "a tick, its count, or a minus keeps nothing and says the press's words" holds only when nothing else is typed. ROADMAP 37 says all of it. (4) **This entry still named what review replaced**: the helper bullet now gives `reloadForRelease(error, person, keep?)`, `reloadKeepingWords(person, words, announce)`, `stale-build-kept` and `forgetOthersKept`, and the e2e sentence the availability's own words. (5) **Issue 37 said every action of waiter mode**: it now says the order actions listed above it, and 111's examples add the menu editor's "נגמר" and saves and the stations' actions, which still fail as before.
