- After the second review (twenty-one findings, several the same issue seen from two lenses; all real once read in the code, two narrower than written; each fixed the smallest way that holds, with the tests that prove it): (1) **A note and what was kept weren't tied to a business** (three findings). The business is the device's cookie, not the address, so a reload could open another business, switched in another tab meanwhile, at the same address (`/schedule/availability`, a private chat), put the first one's week or words back there, and tell the person to send them. The frame now takes the business it was drawn for (`<ReleaseNote userId businessId>`: the app's `business.id`, the design shell's "demo"), and the person it hands the presses is `pressedBy(userId, businessId)` (`app/stale-build.ts`, new), so the note, what a press keeps and the chat's words are that person's in that business alone: another business's frame says nothing of them and lets them go (its "לוחצים שוב" would press in the wrong business), and its screen at the same address takes nothing back. The helper's logic is unchanged. (2) **Waiter mode's held fixes loaded the page by their timer** (two findings). החזרה, a line's cancel and "על חשבון הבית", and the order's cancel wait 8 seconds for their "ביטול" (`useHeldWrite`) and go by its time, a hidden page or the screen left, so a page loaded for one of them moved a keyboard's or a screen reader's focus while the person did something else, with the press's words only. `OrdersModeProvider` now leaves those four out of `reloadOnRelease` (`const { takeBack, cancelLine, compLine, cancelOrder, ...pressed } = actions`): one that meets the release fails at once in its own words (`fixFailureWords`), the line back, as before, and the next press loads the page. A takeaway's name, saved as its field is left, now sends a held fix first, as "הוגש" does, so the fix no longer goes with the page unsaid. (3) **"סיום התיקון" left a count put back unsaved**: a count put back after a release has no timer of its own, and `finishFix` saved only counts with one; it now also saves each count that differs from its item as saved. (4) **The chat's release line hid the composer's other words** (two findings): a PDF too big, a picture that can't be read or didn't go were set and never drawn nor heard. They now show after the line, a change of their own in its live region. (5) **The frame's note** (two findings): its live region arrived a moment before its words, so a screen reader could miss them; the frame now says them through the page's announcer (`@ui/announcer`, there from the root layout's first drawing), and its toast only shows them (`quiet`). The screens' longer words stay 8 seconds (`LONG_WORDS_MS`), the press's own 4. (6) **The focus after the page loaded** (two findings): what came back takes the focus the page loaded starts without, once it is back: the list's field, count or tick where the change was made (`at`, kept with what was typed; `typedBack` gives it only for an item of the list), brought into view; the availability's Send; the takeaway's name. The list's comment sheet, opened again by the reload, gives the focus back to its item's comment button as it closes (`returnFocus`, by `commentOf`), not to the page's body. (7) **The chat's second Send read the line twice**: it no longer says it again; "טעינה מחדש" takes the focus, after the composer's own move to its field, its description the line. (8) **Tests** (seven findings): `typedToKeep` moved into `app/(app)/lists/typed-back.ts`, pure, with `typed`, `countText` and `ItemChange`, and is unit-tested branch by branch (`typed-back.test.ts`, 7 new: a note, a reading with its minus, a count and the ticked item's own left out, a comment saved or still in its sheet, "typed" and "pressTyped", a fix, the item as it was before the change); `app/stale-build.test.ts` (4 new: the business); `e2e/release.ts`'s watcher counts each time words come in, shown in a status region or said by the announcer, so "once" can fail; `e2e/release.spec.ts` asserts the note said and shown exactly once, the focus back, the note gone at the next press well before its time, nothing sent once the page drew, and adds two tests of the frame alone, its note left before the page loads (no screen answers: the press's words after the wait; a press during the wait: nothing); `e2e/clock-release.spec.ts` reads the announcer; `e2e/chat.spec.ts`: the second Send's focus, and an announcement as Shiran whose switch comes back in the field and across "טעינה מחדש"; `e2e/orders.spec.ts`: a takeaway's name on the demo's real /orders comes back to its field, focused and unsaved (phone only, nothing written). `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts`: the four held writes left out, `<ReleaseNote userId businessId>`, `pressedBy` in the frame, the list's keeping in typed-back.ts. (9) **The PR body's test summary** didn't match the specs: the note test now saves a second time within the minute (the list's own words, no second reload) and the shift test runs axe, so what it says of the four is true; it still has to say the chat's e2e, and the new orders, chat and frame tests, run first in CI. ROADMAP: 37 says the business, the held fixes, the focus and the chat's line; "מה לא נבדק" says what is in e2e now. ## 2026-11-28 · chat · closing the pilot, PR 3: the new-business wizard asks about Shabbat

- On chat/wizard-shabbat from origin/main a0158c82 (#280). The pilot-closing plan's PR 3 only (section 1, A): a business open on Saturday (a shop, a hotel, a factory on shifts) started closed on Shabbat, since `closedOnShabbat` defaults to true (`modules/scheduling/manifest.ts:57`), no template sets it, and the console's wizard never asked (template, details, colour, staff). The setting could already be changed (the console's settings page, `/manage/scheduling`); what was missing was the question. No migration, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component (`FieldRow` with a `Switch`, as the settings form and the documents' send form compose them), no change to `seed.sql`, `hosted-demo.sql`, the templates or the demo door. PR 16's card (Shabbat answered) is not here. No customer is named anywhere.
- **The question**, `app/(admin)/admin/new/new-business-form.tsx`: the details step gets a second card under name, slug and address, one row: "העסק פתוח בשבת", with its hint read with the switch (`aria-describedby`): "אם כן, אפשר לתת זמינות גם לשבת, המילוי מהזמינות משבץ בה, ורשימות עם זמנים קבועים נשלחות בה כמו בכל יום. אפשר לשנות אחר כך בהגדרות העסק, במתג „סגור בשבת”." (After the check, below.) It starts from the template's answer (`shownShabbat`: the person's, else `shabbatAnswerOf(template)`, closed in all four), and once the person switches it, the answer stays when another template is picked (`Values.shabbat` is empty until they do). A hidden `shabbat` input sends "open" or "closed". The summary gets a row "בשבת": "פתוח" or "סגור" ("לא מולא" before a template is picked). An error line under the card for `errors.shabbat`, which only a form other than the wizard's can earn.
- **The schema**, `@domain/business`: `shabbat` in `NEW_BUSINESS_FIELDS` and in `NEW_BUSINESS_STEPS[1]` (`name`, `slug`, `address`, `shabbat`), `SHABBAT_ANSWERS`, `ShabbatAnswer` and `shabbatAnswerOf`. `parseNewBusiness` reads it, trimmed, into `NewBusiness.closedOnShabbat` (open false, closed true). None or empty is null, the template's own: a wizard left open across a release sends no answer, and its business opens as before. Anything else is `invalid`, on the details step (`firstStepWithError` 1). `@domain/templates`: `TEMPLATE_CLOSED_ON_SHABBAT`, each template's `closedOnShabbat` as its row leaves it, true for all four.
- **The save**, `app/(admin)/admin/new/actions.ts`: `public.create_business` takes no settings (newest in `20261128000200_invite_links_pending.sql`), and passing the answer there would need a migration, so the action saves it once the business is open (`saveShabbat`), through the settings engine as the console's settings page (`saveBusinessSettingsAction`) and `/manage/scheduling` do: it reads the business's rows (`getBusinessModulesForTeam`), checks the change at the team's level (`checkSettingsChange`) and writes it with `setModuleSettings`, whatever the template gave (After the check, below). `set_module_settings` then writes the `module.settings` line in the audit log, from what the template gave to the answer, with the onboarder or admin who opened the business as its actor. A save that fails (refused, or the read throws) leaves the business open, so the checklist opens anyway, with `&shabbat=unsaved`: the "נפתח" card adds "התשובה על השבת לא נשמרה בגלל תקלה. אפשר לקבוע אותה בהגדרות העסק, במתג „סגור בשבת”." (`admin.setup.opened.shabbatUnsaved`, read only with `created`), and only the error's message is logged. A business that didn't open (slug taken, a number on another staff, a failure) saves nothing.
- **The words** (he and en): `admin.newBusiness.fields.shabbat.{label,hint}`, `summary.shabbat`, `summary.shabbatAnswer.{open,closed}`, `errors.shabbat.invalid`, and `admin.setup.opened.shabbatUnsaved`.

(2) The plan's hint said "והסידור ממלא גם אותה" and "בניהול הסידור". - After the check (four findings, all real): (1) **A kept answer left no trace that it was asked.** `saveShabbat` skipped an answer the template already gives, and no template stores `closedOnShabbat`, so a business the wizard opened closed looked in the data like one opened before the wizard asked: no key in its scheduling settings and no `module.settings` line, and PR 16's "Shabbat answered" would have had nothing to read. The skip was the action's own; `set_module_settings` writes the patch and its line whatever the value. The early return is gone (with its `readModuleSettings`), so the answer is always saved through `checkSettingsChange` and `setModuleSettings`, and a form with no answer still writes nothing. This entry's default (2) had the cost of this wrong: the line goes from nothing (`from: { closedOnShabbat: null }`) to the answer, and `/manage/activity` reads a null `from` as the module's default and leaves out a change that ends there (`withoutNoChange` in `@domain/activity`, covered by "knows a setting never set began at its default"), so the owner sees no line for closed; default (2) now says so. Tests: "saves the answer when it is the template's too" expects `{ closedOnShabbat: true }`, `false` and `true` saved, in that order, where the old test expected nothing; the restaurant in `new-business.spec.ts` now expects `closedOnShabbat: true` and `settingsAudited: 1`. The Save and Tests bullets, point (4), `actions.ts`'s comment, `packages/db/README.md` and the ROADMAP's console row say the same. (2) **The wizard asks "open", the settings page says "closed".** The hint and `admin.setup.opened.shabbatUnsaved` sent the team to the business settings, where the same setting is the switch "סגור בשבת" (`modules.scheduling.settings.closedOnShabbat.label`), on where the wizard's is off. Both now name it: "…אפשר לשנות אחר כך בהגדרות העסק, במתג „סגור בשבת”." and "…אפשר לקבוע אותה בהגדרות העסק, במתג „סגור בשבת”." (en "…with the “Closed on Shabbat” switch."). Default (4) adds the alternative, one name for both. (3) **The hint left out the lists.** The same setting holds scheduled lists back from candle lighting to havdalah and sends no list notifications on Shabbat, only while the business is closed (`closedOnShabbat` in `app/(app)/lists/lists.ts`, `private.closes_on_shabbat` in `20261110001300_list_schedules.sql`). The hint now says "ורשימות עם זמנים קבועים נשלחות בה כמו בכל יום" ("זמנים קבועים", as the lists editor names them), en "and lists with set times go out on it as on any day". The Shabbat city is left out of it: `/manage` asks for one only while the business is closed, and the hint is about what being open adds. (4) **Point (2) put the settings row right under the "נפתח" card**; in `business-view.tsx` the checklist comes first and the row under it (and on tablet and desktop the people sit beside them): now "the row under the checklist, below the 'נפתח' card". It now says what `saveShabbat` does: saved even when it is the template's, so the business says it was answered (a stored `closedOnShabbat` and a `module.settings` line); null, from a form with no answer, writes nothing and keeps the template's. In `business-view.tsx` the status is now the card's title and body, and the line beside them is an alert of its own (`role="alert"`, said as it is drawn, as the view-as failure on the same page does), still in the card and looking the same, so no live region sits inside another. (3) **The real page was never opened with `?shabbat=unsaved`.** Only the twin, which works out its own flag, showed the line. `new-business.spec.ts`, once the café exists, read only: its checklist from the wizard (`?created=1`) has no line; `?created=1&shabbat=unsaved` has it as an alert, with the card's title in the status and the line not; `?shabbat=unsaved` alone has no card and no line. ## 2026-11-27 · chat · closing the pilot, PR 26: a phone too old for the app says so

- On chat/old-browser from origin/main 2f4ecc80 (#277). The pilot-closing plan's PR 26 only (section A, after PR 1a): a browser older than the app needs says so at the top of every page, signed out too (`/enter`, `/sign-in`, `/join`, the clock station), in plain words, with CSS only. No migration, no new npm package, no change to `next.config.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`, `seed.sql` or CI. No customer is named anywhere.
- **The floor, checked first, and where the plan was wrong.** The plan put it at Chrome 120 for `:dir()`. The served CSS has no `:dir()`: Turbopack runs Lightning CSS with Next's default targets (Chrome 111; there is no browserslist), and it rewrites every `rtl:`/`ltr:` variant (and `ltrInputClass`'s `[:dir(rtl)>&]`) into `:is(:lang(ae),…,:lang(he),…)` or `:not(:lang(…))`. Seen in the stylesheet a CI e2e trace saved on 15.11 (four `:lang(ae)`, no `:dir(`), and again by running `app/globals.css` through Tailwind's compile and optimize, as `@tailwindcss/turbopack` does in production, then lightningcss 1.33 with those targets: two `:dir(` before, none after. Tailwind's own pass keeps `:dir()` (it excludes `DirSelector`); Turbopack's doesn't. So the highest need is relative colours, `rgb(from …)`, which the accent's hover and press are made of: Chrome and Edge 119, Safari and iOS 16.4, Firefox 128, Samsung Internet 25 (caniuse-lite 1.0.30001826 in node_modules; partial until Chrome 131 and Safari 18 only for a `currentcolor` origin, which the app doesn't use). Below it: Next 16's Chrome, Edge and Firefox 111 and Safari 16.4 (`supported-browsers.md:8-11`), Tailwind 4's `@property` (Safari 16.4, Firefox 128), `color-mix()` (Chrome 111, Safari 16.2, Firefox 113), `toSpliced` (`availability-sheets.tsx:213`: Chrome 110, Safari 16, Firefox 115), a regex lookbehind in the imports and the bank field (Safari 16.4), `structuredClone`, `findLast`, `Object.hasOwn` and `Intl.DisplayNames`; `OffscreenCanvas`, `BarcodeDetector`, the wake lock and `audioSession` are tested for before use. The condition is `@supports not (color: rgb(from red r g b))` alone, not the plan's `not (selector(:dir(rtl)) and …)`, which would tell Chrome 119, which draws the app, that it is too old. The rule's comment says all this.
- **Changed in CLAUDE.md:** it said `rtl:`/`ltr:` follow the element's real direction even inside a nested `dir` block; in the build they follow `lang`. Its line now says they are written on `:dir()` but served as `:lang()` while Next's targets include Chrome 111, so a nested `dir` block that should flip them also sets its `lang` (and `app/globals.css`'s comment on the variants says the same, since the second review). Today the two always go together (Hebrew right to left, English left to right, the gallery switches both), so nothing shows it; a `dir="ltr"` block in Hebrew with a direction variant inside would. Keeping `:dir()` (`experimental.lightningCssFeatures` excluding `dir-selector`) would make the old line true and raise the floor to Chrome 120 (default (1), below): the comment says to add `or (not selector(:dir(rtl)))` then, and `tooling/old-browser.test.ts` fails on any such change to the targets until the condition moves with it.
- **Hidden, and shown with no script.** The notice carries Tailwind's `hidden`, which is in `@layer utilities`; the one rule in `app/globals.css`, outside any layer, gives `[data-old-browser]` `display: block` under the condition, and an unlayered rule beats a layered one whatever the specificity. A browser too old for cascade layers (Chrome 98, Safari 15.3, Firefox 96) drops every `@layer` block, so it never applies `hidden` and shows the notice; every browser too old for `selector()` is older still, so the plan's caveat that such a browser keeps it hidden no longer holds, and the condition needs no `selector()` anyway. Not the HTML `hidden` attribute: Tailwind's preflight gives `[hidden]` `display: none !important` inside a layer, which no unlayered rule beats, so Safari 15.4 to 16.3 would never have shown it. Checked through the same pipeline: the rule comes out unchanged, at the top level, with `.hidden` in `@layer utilities`.
- **The notice**, `@ui/old-browser-notice` (`OldBrowserNotice`: a title, a paragraph each of what to do, `shown` for the gallery, `OLD_BROWSER_ATTRIBUTE`): a floating card (`bg-surface`, `shadow-float`, a `TriangleAlert` in `text-warning`), a region named by its title, in the flow at the top with the safe area's padding. No z-index (the second review took away `relative z-40`, below): the fixed bars, a toast and a sheet are drawn over it as over the page, and at the top none of them meets it; it never covers the page, which scrolls on under it. Its colours are plain tokens only, never one mixed with `color-mix()` or a relative colour, so it reads where the accent's don't. `app/old-browser.tsx` gives it the words, and `app/layout.tsx` draws it right after the announcer, before the intl provider: on every route group, from the server, no client code. Words, he/en `app.oldBrowser.*`: "הדפדפן במכשיר הזה ישן מדי בשביל האפליקציה", "**באנדרואיד:** מעדכנים את Chrome בחנות Play, ופותחים בו את האפליקציה.", "**באייפון ובאייפד:** צריך iOS 16.4 ומעלה (באייפד: iPadOS 16.4). אפשר לעדכן בהגדרות, בכללי, בעדכון תוכנה." (iOS 16.4 as `notifications.iosVersion` says it), "אם אי אפשר לעדכן, מספרים למי שהזמין אותך.", "אפשר לנסות בכל זאת, אבל ייתכן שחלק מהדברים לא יעבדו." (the words as they are now; the review and the check changed two lines, below).
- **/design**: "דפדפן ישן מדי", after the empty state: the same `OldBrowser` with `shown`, in the gallery's language, and a line saying where it shows (`design.oldBrowser`, `design.oldBrowserHint`).
- Tests. Unit, `packages/ui/old-browser-notice.test.ts` (5): hidden by default with the attribute; drawn when `shown`; the title, the paragraphs and a region named by the title, the icon hidden from a screen reader; in the flow, neither it nor its card positioned or given a z-index, nothing modal or without the pointer; a screen's own spacing in place of its own. Tooling, `tooling/old-browser.test.ts` (8): the rule once, at the top level, its body exactly `[data-old-browser] { display: block; }`; nothing else in the stylesheet names it; the accent's hover and press still use `rgb(from …)`, the floor's reason; no `lightningCssFeatures`, `dir-selector` or browserslist in `next.config.ts`, `package.json` or a file of its own, and the `:dir()` variants as they were checked; the root layout a server file drawing it after the announcer and before the page, hidden; no client code in either file; every colour utility it draws (`bg-surface`, `shadow-float`, `text-ink`, `text-ink-2`, `text-warning`) a plain value in light and in dark; the words (five keys, Chrome and Play, iOS 16.4 in both languages, the three Settings steps, whom to tell, no exclamation mark), each drawn with nothing missing and the platforms in bold. e2e, `e2e/old-browser.spec.ts` (at 390, 820 and 1440, as every spec): the server's HTML of `/sign-in` and `/enter` carries the notice; on `/enter`, `/sign-in`, a `/join` that leads nowhere, `/station`, `/design` and `/design/shell` the browser reads relative colours and the notice is there, once, with its words, and hidden; the same at `/home` after the door, as the demo restaurant's worker; on `/design` the gallery's is shown, its lines and bold platforms, axe on it, then the same in English, while the page's own stays hidden; and in light and dark on `/sign-in` and `/design/shell`, the rule as served (its condition read back from the stylesheet) applied as a browser without relative colours applies it: the notice shows at the top, nothing that takes the pointer and no fixed element that can be seen is drawn over it, on `/design/shell` the first link of the main navigation in sight still takes a trial press, the page's title starts below it, and axe and the width are clean; on a phone made too short for both, the tab bar is drawn over the card and still takes the press.
- Docs: `packages/ui/README.md` (the component, how it hides and shows), ROADMAP known issue 110 (an old phone is not supported, and now says so) and a line under "מה לא נבדק" (only a new Chromium, the rule forced, and `/design`; never a real old phone), this entry. Not run here: `pnpm build`, `pnpm dev`, Playwright (the new spec runs in CI), and a real old phone (Orel's round, section 2 step 10: an iPhone 7 or first SE on iOS 15, or an Android that no longer updates).

## 2026-11-27 · chat · closing the pilot, PR 2: one network sent too many codes, and the screen says so

- On chat/network-limit from origin/main 2f4ecc80 (#277). The pilot-closing plan's PR 2 only (section 1, A): the 21st person joining on the shop's Wi-Fi within a quarter of an hour was shown `/join`'s code step as if a code went (`join/actions.ts:81-83`), and waited for an SMS that never came. One migration, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component, no change to `seed.sql`, `hosted-demo.sql`, `playwright.config.ts` or the demo door. No customer is named anywhere.
- **The database**, `20261128000600_network_limit.sql` (main's newest was …000500, #277's `shift_cap.sql`): `public.note_auth_attempt` replaced with `create or replace`, so its arguments, its `integer` and its grants stay (revoked from public, anon and authenticated; service_role only, restated). A try its caller's own windows hold now answers 100000 more than the caller's own seconds to wait, whatever the number's count; held by both, the caller's is said, with the caller's seconds, never the number's; a try only the number's windows hold answers its seconds, as before. A wait is shorter than its window and no window is longer than a day, so the two never meet. A try counted for its caller only (-1, -2) that the caller's count holds answers the caller's too. The diff against its version in `20261127000200_sign_in_windows.sql` is one constant, two variables, three lines in the window loop and one early return; every other behaviour (0, -1, -2, every window, the wait, the locks, a refused try counted nowhere, hashes, the day's clearing) is unchanged. Two `$$`.
- **Why an integer and not a pair**: production is migrated before its code is released (docs/production.md, "עדכון אחרי שעסק אמיתי עלה"), and an Instant Rollback brings back old code on the new database. The old server parses `z.number().int().min(-2)` and reads any positive answer as too many, so it keeps working unchanged against this function, and the new server reads the old function's waits as the number's and says them as before. Either order is safe; `pnpm db:types` has nothing to change (`Returns: number`).
- **Why the caller's may be said**: its count is the same for every number. Sign-in counts a send to a number with no account as it counts one that went; joining clears only the number's counts (`forget_number_attempts`). Held by both, saying the number's would have told, on joining, a number whose counts were kept (an account) from one whose counts were cleared, so the caller's wins, with its own seconds.
- **The one exception, written down** (migration header, the new pgTAP file, ROADMAP 98, `supabase/README.md`, `packages/db/README.md`): a send the SMS provider or the project's SMS cap refused is given back for the number and the caller (`forget_auth_attempt`), and on sign-in only a number with an account meets that. So the caller's count then differs by account, and the next send from that network may go where, after a number with no account, it would have been held. It tells nothing more: that very send already answered "משהו השתבש" for a number with an account and the code step for one without (known issue 98), and a hold by the caller's count was already visible on sign-in before this PR ("היו יותר מדי ניסיונות"); only which count held it is new, and that is the caller's. On joining every number has its account before its code goes, so a give-back there is alike. So the screen is **not** kept silent after a send given back (the plan's fallback); see defaults.
- **The wrapper**, `@db/auth-limits`: `noteAuthAttempt` now answers a typed `AttemptAnswer` (0, `NO_CODE_WAITING`, `CODE_JUST_SENT`, or `Held = { by: "phone" | "caller"; seconds }`), parsed with Zod: an int from -2 to `HELD_BY_CALLER + DAY`, refusing anything between a day and the offset. `HELD_BY_CALLER = 100_000` is exported.
- **The server**, `app/(auth)/otp.ts`: `count` turns a caller's hold into `NetworkHeld = { error: "tooManyNetwork", minutes }` (`minutesToWait` in `@domain/otp`: rounded up, at least one); the number's stays `tooMany`. `sendSignInCode` and `sendJoinCode` return it; `sendCode`, now only the hiring file's, says it as `tooMany`, as before (the file's words are its own); a check held by the network's count (`checked`, `verifyCode`, `checkCode`) still says `tooMany`, since the line speaks of codes sent. `SignInError` is unchanged; `Refusal<E>` (`{ error?: E; minutes?: undefined } | NetworkHeld`) types each step's state, so minutes only ever come with `tooManyNetwork`.
- **The actions**: sign-in (`actions.ts`) keeps a network hold on its step, with its minutes: the phone step on a send, the code step on a resend; a check drops a network's minutes from the step before. Joining (`join/actions.ts`): on the first send a network hold stays on the invite step and is said (never the code step), while the number's own too many and too soon still read as the code step, word for word; on a resend it is said on the code step.
- **The words**, `auth.signIn.errors.tooManyNetwork` (he and en): "נשלחו הרבה קודים מהרשת הזאת. אפשר לכבות את ה-Wi-Fi ולנסות מהסלולר, או לנסות שוב בעוד {minutes, plural, one {דקה} two {שתי דקות} other {<ltr>{minutes}</ltr> דקות}}." / "Many codes were sent from this network. You can turn off Wi-Fi and try on mobile data, or try again in {minutes, plural, one {a minute} other {<ltr>{minutes}</ltr> minutes}}." The forms draw it with `t.rich` (`<span dir="ltr">`) and say it with `t.markup` (plain, for the announcer): above the press on sign-in's phone step and joining's invite step, as before for any other refusal; on the code step through `CodeField`'s new `errorLine` (the line as drawn; `error` stays the words said and described). The plan named the key `auth.errors.tooManyNetwork`; no `auth.errors` exists, and both forms read `auth.signIn.errors`.
- **Twins**: `/design/screens/sign-in?error=network` (either step, `?step=code`) and `/design/screens/join?error=network` (`JoinView` takes a `start` for it), twelve minutes, from `app/design/screens/refusal-twin.ts`; the three are in `e2e/screens.spec.ts`'s pages (axe, both appearances, three widths).
- **Docs**: docs/production.md ב12 (one line: the screen says it, the number's limit stays silent), ROADMAP (the sign-in row: 20 and 40 per network, said; known issue 98: the exception), `packages/db/README.md`, `supabase/README.md`, this entry.
- Tests. pgTAP `supabase/tests/database/auth_limits_network.test.sql` (30, `plan(30)`: 24 at first, five added after review and one after the second review, below): the signature and `integer` (`function_returns`); the number's own hold answers 720 as before; a fourth number from one network 100870, the same for a number with an account and for one joining just cleared; the number itself not held nor counted; held by both 100510 (the caller's 510, not the number's 750), and 750 from another network; a send within the wait from a full network 100860; a third check from one network 100880, and 0 from another; the exception: two networks with the same three sends, one ending on a number with no account (kept), the other on the account whose send was given back: the next answers 100870 and 0; refused tries counted nowhere, for the network or the number; only the server executes. Existing pgTAP: the three caller holds now expect 100570, 100898 and 100791 (`auth_limits.test.sql`, `auth_limits_windows.test.sql`), plans unchanged. - Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (Biome, `next typegen`, tsc, 5532 tests), the migration's `$$` (2), the test files' (`auth_limits.test.sql` 2 and `auth_limits_windows.test.sql` 8, unchanged; the new file 0), `plan(30)` against its 30 assertions (as committed; the first pass had 24 of 24), every expected number in the new file worked by hand. ## 2026-11-27 · chat · closing the pilot, PR 1a: the clock survives a release

- On chat/stale-build from origin/main ae6a8712 (#276). The pilot-closing plan's PR 1a (section 1, A, PR 1, split in two in its second pass): one client helper and the clock's two catch paths only (the plan's `deploymentId` taken out after review). The other catch paths (availability send, a list item's check, swap and give-away), the chat composer's button and waiter mode's actions are PR 1b, which also closes known issue 37. No migration, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component.
- **Why**: a release may give the server actions new ids, and a page left open from the release before then asks for one the server no longer has. Next.js answers `x-nextjs-action-not-found` and the client throws `UnrecognizedActionError` (`next/dist/client/components/router-reducer/reducers/server-action-reducer.js:103-105`); the clock said "לא הצלחנו לרשום את זה. בדקו את החיבור ונסו שוב." at every press until the app was opened again, and an iPhone's home-screen app resumes the old page. A shift that starts before a release and ends after it met it on "יציאה".
- **The helper**, `app/stale-build.ts` (no React): `isStaleBuild(error)` is `unstable_isUnrecognizedActionError` from `next/navigation` (exported in the installed 16.4.6, `navigation.d.ts:126`; an `instanceof` on Next.js's class, so an error that only says the name doesn't count). `reloadForRelease(error, person)`: for that error only, unless the tab loaded itself again less than a minute ago (`stale-build-reloaded` in sessionStorage, the time of the last one; a time after now, the phone's clock moved back, holds nothing back), it keeps a note (`stale-build-updated`: its time and the person whose press it was) and then the time, calls `window.location.reload()` and returns true. Anything else, a reload within the minute, or storage that can't be read or written returns false, and the press says its failure as before; a note kept without its guard is taken away again, so neither is kept halfway (After the check, below). `untilReloaded()` is a promise that never settles, for the press to wait on while the page goes. `takeUpdatedNote(person)` is true once for that person's note left in the last 5 minutes, and the note goes either way.
- **The clock**, `app/(app)/clock/clock-panel.tsx`: the screen's own press (`run`, its catch at 206-212 at ae6a8712) and a code's punch (`punchWith`, 260-262, the camera's and both sheets') each `if (reloadForRelease(error, userId)) return untilReloaded();` after `unstable_rethrow`. So the press stays on its way while the page loads again: the circle or the button busy, every other button waiting, the camera or the sheet busy, and no failure said for a moment. Nothing sends the press again: after the reload the person presses, and the time recorded is that press. On mount the panel takes the note for `data.userId` and shows its own `StatusToast`: "האפליקציה התעדכנה, והלחיצה לא נרשמה. לוחצים שוב." (`app.updated.toast`; "The app was updated, and your press wasn't recorded. Press again.").
- **No deploymentId** (taken out after review, below): `next.config.ts` sets none and says why in a comment, and `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts` keeps it so. A navigation and an action's answer from another release load the page whole by the build id, as on main.
- **The stale tab's list**, `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts`: the reload is listed with its reason (the same page, for the same person in the same session, once a minute at most, never the press again; the server never ran the press, and should the session have ended meanwhile, the reload comes from the app's own page, so the proxy keeps nothing of it). A new check counts every `reloadForRelease(` in app/, packages/ and modules/: the clock panel's two, each `return untilReloaded()`, and nowhere else until PR 1b lists its own.
- Tests. Unit: `app/stale-build.test.ts` (16: Next.js's own error told from a plain `Error`, a `TypeError`, an object or a string named the same; the reload and both marks; any other failure loads nothing; once a minute, a press at 59.999 s says the failure, at 60 s loads again; a guard after now or not a time holds nothing back; storage unreadable or full loads nothing and leaves no note halfway; `untilReloaded` never settles; the note said once, the guard staying; no note, a note 5 minutes old, one just within, one that doesn't read or is after now, storage unreadable and a note that can't be taken away all say nothing; the note's words, he and en, say the press wasn't recorded), `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts` (the listed reload, the new count, no `deploymentId` in next.config.ts). No e2e: it would take two builds of the app, one with an action renamed; the PR should say so.
- Docs: ROADMAP (known issue 37: the clock no longer meets it, the rest in the next PR; "מה לא נבדק": a release with the clock open, tested only in unit tests), this entry.
- Checked here: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (Biome, `next typegen`, tsc, 5510 tests; `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts` timed out once at 5 s on a cold disk in a run of five files, green alone in 0.7 s and in the whole run). Not run here: `pnpm build`, `next start`, `pnpm dev`, Playwright.
- Checked against the code at ae6a8712, where it differs from the plan: (1) The plan's step 2 sets `deploymentId` so that a navigation that meets a newer deployment does a full reload. A navigation already does: the router compares the server's build id with its own and loads the page whole on a difference (`fetch-server-response.js:163`). So does an action's answer from another release, which carries the build id as `b` (`server-action-reducer.js:169-170`); a `deploymentId` without Skew Protection takes that away, so it isn't set (After review, below). (2) Next.js keeps an action's id for up to 14 days when its code is unchanged (`02-guides/server-actions.md:174`), so not every release breaks a page left open; the plan's local demo needs its renamed action. (3) The plan's line numbers, the export and the version hold.
- Defaults applied, to ask Orel: (1) While the page loads again, the press stays busy and says nothing; the alternative is the old failure for a moment. (2) The toast is the clock's own, since the reload lands back on `/clock`; PR 1b may move it to the app's frame when other screens reload too. (3) The note waits 5 minutes for the page that says it, so a reload that went to the door doesn't say "עודכנה" much later, and only the person whose press it was is told (After the check, below). (4) Storage that can't be read or written: no reload, as there is no guard against a loop; the press says the failure as before. (5) One reload a minute, timed by the phone's clock. (6) The note's words, "האפליקציה התעדכנה, והלחיצה לא נרשמה. לוחצים שוב." (en "The app was updated, and your press wasn't recorded. Press again."): what didn't happen, then what to do, as the clock's other failures say it; in place of the plan's "האפליקציה עודכנה. אפשר ללחוץ שוב.", which named the cause and not the result. The alternative is a line under the circle that stays until the next press.
- After review (three findings, all real; the second goes with the first): (1) **`deploymentId` turned off Next.js's check on a server action's answer.** With it set, `maybeAppendBuildIdToRSCPayload` (`app-render.js:191-197`) leaves the build id `b` out of every answer, actions' included; the `x-nextjs-deployment-id` header that takes its place is never on an action's answer (`app-page-runtime.js:1268`, and the routes-manifest rule needs the `rsc: 1` header, which `fetchServerAction` doesn't send), so at `server-action-reducer.js:169-170` the id is `undefined` and nothing is discarded. A press the next release still knows (its ids last up to 14 days) ran there, and its answer, the page after `refresh()`, was drawn into the old page's code, a mixed-version screen when the release changed the clock's or the frame's client code. Without it, `b` is the build's own: an answer from another build is discarded and the router loads the page whole (`fetch-server-response.js:163`), as on main. Taken out: the line and its comment in `next.config.ts` (a comment says why none is set: Skew Protection's `NEXT_DEPLOYMENT_ID` is read by Next.js itself, `config.js:731-733`), `packages/domain/deployment-id.ts`, its test and the README paragraph; `app/stale-build.ts`'s comment says the build id does it; a check in `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts` fails on a `deploymentId:` in next.config.ts. ROADMAP's "מה לא נבדק" names a press the new release still knows in place of the first Vercel build with the id. The LOG's point that it "does nothing for server actions" was wrong, and the waiter's send in known issue 37 holds across a release because it is a route handler, not because its action's id held. (2) `deploymentIdOf` checked `NEXT_DEPLOYMENT_ID` though Next.js takes it as it is whatever the config says: gone with (1). (3) The note named the cause, not that the press wasn't recorded, where every other press the clock didn't record says so: now "האפליקציה התעדכנה, והלחיצה לא נרשמה. לוחצים שוב." (en "The app was updated, and your press wasn't recorded. Press again."), quoted so in ROADMAP's known issue 37 and above, default (6); a test in `app/stale-build.test.ts` holds both. Checked: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (5508 tests).
- After the check: (two findings, both real) (1) **The cleanup for a half-kept mark took away a note that was never there, and left the guard.** `reloadForRelease` wrote the guard first and the note second, so the only half state was a guard without its note: the catch's `removeItem` of the note found nothing, and the guard held the next minute's press back with no page loaded again. The note is now written first and the guard second, so the half state left is a note without its guard, which the same `removeItem` takes away; the header comment and the line above say so. The test refuses each mark in turn and holds that nothing is kept (it failed with the old order, and with the `removeItem` taken out), that a press with storage working again loads the page, and that an earlier note goes too. (2) **The note outlived the session.** Should the person's session have ended with the old clock open, the reload went to the door, and whoever signed in next on that tab and opened the clock within 5 minutes was told their press wasn't recorded. The note now names the person, `<time> <userId>`: `reloadForRelease(error, person)` and `takeUpdatedNote(person)`, with `ClockData.userId` (the signed-in person, from `clockAccess`; `"demo"` in `/design`'s mock) passed by the clock panel. A note for someone else goes saying nothing. Tests: someone else signed in says nothing and the note goes; notes that name nobody or someone else say nothing; `tooling/stale-tab.test.ts`'s reason says the note is the person's alone, and its count holds each press as `reloadForRelease(error, userId)`. `app/stale-build.test.ts` has 18 tests. Checked: `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (5510 tests).
- After the second review (six findings, all real; three are the same one): (1) **`untilReloaded()` never settled** (findings 1, 3 and 6). React ties every later transition to an async action still pending (`entangleAsyncAction`, and `requestTransitionLane` handing out its lane, in Next's react-dom), so should the reload not replace the page (the browser's stop or Escape before its answer, a back gesture during it), the circle, the camera and the sheet stayed busy, the sheet held, and the tab bar's and the back gesture's navigations never committed. It now settles after `RELOAD_WAIT_MS` (15 s), on `pageshow` with `persisted`, or on `popstate`, whichever comes first, and takes the note away, as no page was loaded again to say it; the minute's guard stays. Both callers `await untilReloaded();` and go on to their failure as before (`run`: `refused()`, and `failed` said once the press is over; `punchWith`: `{ ok: false, failure: "failed" }`), so the transition ends. (2) **The comments said too much** (finding 2). An answer from another build is dropped, and the page loads whole only when the action reads the page again or redirects, as every clock action does (`refresh()`). Skew Protection sets the deployment id through `NEXT_DEPLOYMENT_ID` whatever next.config.ts says (`config.js:731-733`), which leaves `b` out of an action's answer again, and holds a page to its release only for its maximum age. ## 2026-11-27 · chat · scheduling: at most 50 people on a shift, in the database too (the known issue from measuring)

- On chat/shift-cap from origin/main 4969d69e (#275). ROADMAP 82's known issue, found by #275's second review while measuring a business of a few hundred people: the database counted nobody on a shift, so a request built by hand, or two screens adding people to one shift under way, could leave it with more than 50, and copying that week then failed. The whole plan in one PR: one migration, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component, no change to `seed.sql`, `hosted-demo.sql`, `e2e/` or `playwright.config.ts`. No customer is named anywhere.
- **One constant**, `MAX_PEOPLE_ON_A_SHIFT = 50` in `@domain/staffing`, beside `MAX_SHIFTS_IN_A_WEEK`: the pattern's `needed`, `scheduledShift`'s `needed` and `assignees`, `shiftDetailsProblem`, the builder sheet's Stepper (`shift-sheet.tsx`), the shifts editor's `NEEDED_MAX` (`manage/shifts-editor.tsx`), the measuring shape's `CAPS.needed` and the scale test read it. The words that already say "בין 1 ל-50" write the number as before (none had a placeholder).
- **The database**, `20261128000500_shift_cap.sql` (main's newest was …000400; no branch in the local refs uses …000500): `private.refuse_crowded_shifts()`, plpgsql, security definer (the count must see every row whatever role inserts, and the lock needs update rights), `search_path = ''`, revoked from public, anon and authenticated, behind two statement-level triggers on `public.shift_assignments` (Postgres takes one event per trigger with transition tables): `shift_assignments_at_most_50` after insert (`new_rows`) and `shift_assignments_moved_at_most_50` after update (`old_rows`, `new_rows`). It takes the shifts the statement put more people on than it took off (every shift an insert added to; on an update, those whose rows after outnumber those before), locks them `for no key update` in order of id, counts, and refuses any past 50: 22023, 'More than 50 people on a shift', hint `full`, DETAIL their ids as a JSON array in order. So every path is held: the week's write (new and edited shifts, `joins`, from a save, a publish and the demo's renewal), the inserts made straight into the table (the measuring seed, the e2e helpers, pgTAP) and anything later. A swap's approval (user_id updated), a position changed and someone taken off pass, a shift already over 50 included, which takes nobody more. Comments on the function and both triggers.
- **At once**: only the week's write adds people today, and it locks the week and then all its shifts for update first, so two writes already take turns. The trigger doesn't rest on it: the lock on the shift rows makes any two statements adding to one shift take turns, and in read committed the one that waited counts after the other committed. For no key update, not for update: every insert holds a key share on its shift through the foreign key, so for update would make two inserts wait for each other. No week row is locked, so the week locks' order (`tooling/running-week-edge.test.ts`) is untouched. Said in the migration.
- **The server**, `app/(app)/schedule/build/actions.ts`: a new failure, `shiftFull`. A week whose `weekSchedule` parse failed on a shift's `assignees` being too long (`crowdedShiftIn(error)` in `@domain/schedule`, a Zod `too_big` at `[i, "assignees"]`) says it instead of `invalid`; people joining a shift under way are counted with everyone on it as the week is stored now (`crowdedByJoins(build.shifts, joins)` in `@domain/running-week`, pure: each person once, ids in any case, a shift not stored counted from its joins alone, the shifts named in the order first joined), before anything is sent; and the database's refusal (`crowdedShifts(error)` in `@db/schedule-errors`, 22023 + `full`, the ids or `[]`) is mapped to the same failure, so a race gets words rather than "לא הצלחנו לשמור".
- **The words**, `app.build.errors.shiftFull` (he and en, `t.rich` with `<ltr>{max}</ltr>` from the constant and the button pressed, `{intent, select, …}` as `edgeClash`, in `failureWords`): for a save "במשמרת יכולים להיות עד 50 אנשים, ולכן הסידור לא נשמר. מורידים אנשים מהמשמרת שיש בה יותר ושומרים שוב.", for a publish "…ולכן הסידור לא פורסם. מורידים אנשים מהמשמרת שיש בה יותר ומפרסמים שוב." (a published week, where people join a shift under way, has no save button), then for both "אם אין כאן משמרת כזאת, נוספו אליה אנשים בינתיים ממסך אחר: רעננו את המסך כדי לראות אותה כמו שהיא עכשיו." The last sentence is for the race, whose screen shows no shift over 50.
- **The test business**: `measure/scale/scale.db.test.ts`'s 51-person save now expects the refusal (`{ code: "22023", hint: "full", message: "More than 50 people on a shift" }`) and prints "51 people on a shift that needs 50: refused by the database (…)"; the 200 shifts with the 50 still pass.

- Guessed: Orel left no work, no `question`, `orel` or `urgent` issue is open, and nobody wrote `בטל`. This run's container could start Docker, so it ran it: `pnpm db:start`, `pnpm measure:seed` (300 people, 13 months, main at `4969d69`), then `pnpm measure:clean`. Local database only; nothing hosted touched.
- The numbers, now in docs/measure.md ("ההרצה הראשונה"): 114 s, every count as the shape planned; the database grew 142 MB (25 to 174), `audit_log` 62 MB of it; about 10.5 MB a month by the seed's arithmetic (the log 4.5, hours 2.1, breaks 1.3, chat 1.3, assignments 0.9), the bell's lines about 4.3 MB held; payslips at 150 KB about 44 MB a month, so the 50 MB document space fills in about a month at 300 people (8 to 16 months for a pilot of 20 to 40).
- Found: a punch that waits for confirmation costs about 56 ms in the database at 300 people against about 3 ms at 7, nearly all in `audit_log_notifies`: `private.notify_for`'s clock case calls `private.members_holding(b, 'clock.edit')`, which asks `private.holds_perm` for every member, about 313 `private.role_gives` calls a punch (`explain analyze` and `pg_stat_xact_user_functions` on 20 punches each, rolled back). Scanned punches only resolve, so they don't take this path. That is why the seed's 95-waiting step took 69 s. Fine at 300, but linear in the team; the fix (the roles that give a permission read once, not once a member) is a migration for a day with Orel, not a guess.

- After review (seven findings, all real; two were the same one): **the rows went to SQL encoded twice**: `runPart` sent `JSON.stringify(chunk)`, `$1::jsonb` made postgres.js take the parameter as jsonb and stringify it again, so the seed would have stopped at 10-people's first step ("cannot call jsonb_to_recordset on a scalar") and the database job gone red; now `tx.json(chunk)`, and `tx.json(vector)` for the md5 vector in the database test, the SQL unchanged; `parts.test.ts` checks the rows go as a typed JSON parameter, chunked, and that nothing in measure/ casts a JSON string to jsonb. **The cost a month** divided every table by the history's months, though the chat covers 90 days and the documents their months of payslips: `spansOf` gives each table the months its rows cover in the shape (the hours, breaks and log from the history's first week; shifts and assignments over their weeks; the chat over its 90 days; documents and recipients over the payslip months, the procedure with them), each row and the label naming what it was divided by; the bell's lines are out of the MB a month, given as what the app would hold under its cleaning (180 days, read ones 90), the waiting hours' clock.confirm backlog left out (`linesHeld`); `cost.test.ts` (2). **The fixed counts held only at the test's anchor**: on the clock now takes whoever has arrived (10 minutes early to 5 late) in a shift still running, two hours or more left first, then the most time left; the punchers are taken first, the ones off today by choice and the rest off today's shifts, before the hours; live swaps stay on this week, fewer in its last three hours, said in the docs and LOG. Swept here every 15 minutes through a week: at 300, 15 on the clock and 100 punchers at every anchor the run takes (as few as 6 and 89 before), at 600 30 and 200, at 30 and 100 the punchers always and nobody on the clock while closed; `shape.test.ts` sweeps 48 anchors at 300 (four-hourly, and the hours that fell short) and fails on the old code. **The history's log came after the anchor's own rows**: 40-weeks is now 92-weeks and `record_absence` its own 93-absences, both after 90-log (nothing before needs the weeks: no entry ties to a shift, and the bell's history of publishing names its weeks in its lines alone), the expectations moved with them, and the database test checks every clock.confirm and clock.correct id is below every schedule.publish and clock.absence_add id; the named role's two rows still come first, as a business's first. **The work counts** move to the measuring run, said in the plan against the code. **The example names** didn't exist: the docs and the LOG take theirs from `nameOf` ("נועם בדיקה 017", "סמאח ב׳ 018", "יוסף-אליעזר בדיקה-ארוכה 025"), and a double name's second is of the first's form (`SECOND_NAMES` by form, with a test). read against every trigger on the tables the seed writes and every function it calls (only create_business_role, set_role_permission, publish_week_schedule and record_absence write the log; role.create and role.permission tell only members holding the role, and none does yet); ## 2026-11-26 · chat · inviting from a list (הזמנה מרשימה), part 5: the Team screen

- On chat/import-team from 568a1ba8, the head of chat/import-console (part 4; it held main fce2fea with parts 1–3). Part 4 has since merged as #272 (7bb37a92, the same tree as 568a1ba8), and main is merged here, so the pull request shows part 5 alone. The plan's PR 5 of section 7 ("הזמנה מרשימה במסך הצוות"), under Orel's decisions of 25.11.2026: owners and managers paste on /team, after the console; each person's link is made at the moment of sending ("יצירת קישור", then WhatsApp straight to the number, or a copy); the app sends nothing. This completes the MVP (parts 1–5). No migration, no SQL, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component (so no /design gallery entry): the send list is part 2's `SendLinkRow` and its lists. Nothing of PR 6's or later: the wizard still reads its own staff list, no department, no file.
- **The route**, `app/(app)/team/import/page.tsx`: not found unless the person may invite one at a time, exactly as /team/invite asks (`invitableRoles`, then `invitesOpen`, so never a hosted demo, #80), and unless a staff role is left them to give (`listRoleOf` over the roles below): owners and accountants are invited one at a time. The roles are the invite form's (`inviteChoices` with `currentOwnerOnlyRoles` and `currentGivableNamedRoles`, those only an owner gives shown with why), worded as the business words its roles (`translator(currentWording(), "app.roles")`); the team is the Team screen's own (`currentTeam`, under RLS) through the new `teamKnownOf`. The view is part 1's `PeopleImportView` in team mode, keyed by person and business as /team is, with the bound actions, the way back to /team, and the send list's actions (`firstLinkAction`, `resendInviteAction`, the business's name, WhatsApp to the number). The title goes through `translator`, never next-intl's server translator.
- **The actions**, `app/(app)/team/import/actions.ts`: `importPeopleAction(business, input)` and `undoPeopleImportAction(business, importId)`, bound on the page to the business it was drawn for. In `changer`'s order with the invite form's words: no member or no team.edit `noPermission`; a bound id that is no id `changed`; another business `businessChanged`; a session viewing as someone `viewOnly`. Then Zod at the boundary (`peopleImportRows`: `changed`, nothing asked, nothing logged), then each row as the invite form would take it from this person (`parseTeamInvite` against the live named roles, the same name, number, role and named role back; a named role gone `roleGone`; a built-in role only an owner gives, which `parseTeamInvite` doesn't check, `changed`), then `importPeopleToTeam`. Its refusals keep their words (`tooManyOpen` with `open`, `demo`, `notAllowed`, `givesPay`, `roleGone`, `tooMany`, `changed`, `failed`); `elsewhere`, which the Team screen's function never answers, would be `failed`, never said (#80). Done: `revalidatePath("/team")` and `refresh()`, as part 4's, and the answer rebuilt key by key, so no link or token rides along. A throw is `failed`, its message alone logged. The undo: `isUuid`, `undoTeamImport`, `{ok, cancelled, kept}`.
- **The done screen's send list** (team mode only, `send` on the view): `done-links.ts` (`doneLinksOf`, no React) lists, from the database's answer, every row it made, updated or found the same with its invite, in the role it ends with (`writtenRoleOf`, else the invite the screen read, else none said), and every line the screen itself left out as already waiting with no link that the viewer may change (the wizard's manager pasted with no role), once each; and, apart, each line whose invite's link ran out that the viewer may renew. `done-send.tsx`: `DoneLinks` puts part 2's `InviteLinks` and `PendingLinks` around the whole view, so a list taken back and kept again with its "ביטול" still shows the links made, never "יצירת קישור" again; `DoneSendList` is /team's own rows (`PendingList` on a phone and a tablet, `PendingTableRows` in a table on a computer, each with `data-pending-links`), so it is the same flow as #266: "יצירת קישור", WhatsApp to the number or the copy, the row says which, and the focus goes on to the next person in the shape on screen; `DoneExpired` folds those whose link ran out into one row ("הקישור הקודם פג", "אדם אחד מהרשימה", show and hide), each with part 2's `ResendLink` ("קישור חדש"), `app.team.invites.expiredHint` under them. Layout: one under the other on a phone; on a tablet the done card at the start, the list at the end; on a computer the card above and the table across under it (side by side, the link column wouldn't fit). The done screen has no accent button: every "יצירת קישור" is secondary, as on /team. A row whose role the screen can't know (an invite made meanwhile elsewhere) says none, and /team's list drops its " · " then.
- **The entry rows.** `TeamView` takes `importHref`, drawn only where invites are, for whoever edits: a quiet `ListGroup`/`ListRow` with `ListPlus`, "הזמנה מרשימה · יש רשימה? כמה אנשים בבת אחת", at the foot of the invites on a phone and a tablet and under the table on a computer (hidden with the invites under a file filter's choice). `TeamInviteView` takes it too, above the form. Both pages pass it only when the import would open for the viewer (`importsPeople`, the new rule, and `invitesOpen`), so no row leads to a page not found; "הזמנה לצוות" stays the screen's one main action.
- **@domain/people-import.** `importsPeople(choices)`: a staff role among the invite form's choices that isn't an owner's alone. `teamKnownOf(team, viewer, named, ownerOnly)`: the Team screen's `KnownTeam`, an invite `touchable` only when the viewer would make its link now (a manager's or a worker's, `canResendInvite`, and a role that sees pay only for an owner), as /team's `resendable` offers it.
- **Words** (he and en, same keys and placeholders): `app.team.import.entry`, `entryHint`, `done.expired.title`, `done.expired.count`. The send list's are part 2's `app.team.pending.*` and `app.team.link.*`.
- **The twin's send list**, which #266 left for this PR: `/design/shell/team/import?state=done` now shows it under the done card, with the Team twin's stand-in for each link (`demoResendAction`) and WhatsApp to nobody (`toNumber: false`, so it asks whom to send to), since a list pasted on the hosted twin may be real. The stand-in hears only the twin's invite id. The twin's invites now have ids of their own across imports, kept as they are updated (they were the row's place, so a second list's first person showed the first list's link). The Team twin and the invite twin draw the entry rows (the invite twin's `?as=paid` none, with no role left).

(4) The e2e account: the manager's line quotes `problem.oneByOneOwners`, and the plan's two further cases the spec leaves out are named with where each is covered. `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (5391 tests). Not run here, as before: `pnpm build`, `pnpm dev`, Playwright, pgTAP.
- After the second review (twelve findings: seven fixed in the code, four that asked only for tests, one left): (1) The fold of those whose link ran out drew initials made of "בלי שם" for an invite with no name, where /team draws the neutral face: `ExpiredRow` keeps the invite's own `name` (null for none) beside `shown`, and `DoneExpired` draws `PersonRow name={row.name}`. (2) The focus moved on to the next person's "יצירת קישור", then to their "וואטסאפ", could land under the tab bar floating over a phone's and a tablet's bottom (WCAG 2.4.11): both take `max-xl:scroll-mb-[calc(env(safe-area-inset-bottom)+6rem)]` in `SendLinkRow`, as the menu editor's arrows do, so on /team too. (3) With links made, the take-back's kept count was said twice: in "ביטול"'s name as it took the focus, and by the announcer after the hold. `TakenBackUndo` tells the screen the count its words last said (`onKept`, kept in `keptSaid`), and the database's count is said after the hold only when it differs. (4) The preview's counts were said unasked 600 ms after a take-back (the lines whose link was made read as invited, so the counts changed with nothing edited): `edits` goes back to 0 when an import succeeds, so the counts are said again only after the next edit. (5) When the held undo failed with a send list, the alert stayed above the card while the focus and the page went to the delete after the list: with a send list it is drawn first among the ways on (`DoneActions`'s `alert`, `md:basis-full`), beside the delete; without one it stays above the card. (6) A link arriving while the take-back held changed "ביטול"'s words, and its status, read whole, said the sentence twice (its words, and its button's name that holds them): an `UndoRow` named by its words (`namedByWords`) is now `aria-atomic="false"`, so a change reads its words alone, on every screen (UndoRow is on /design already; NVDA may still say the focused button's new name). (7) Left, with why: the copy button's own text turns to "הקישור הועתק" while it holds the focus, so NVDA says that name change as well as the announcer. It is `app/copy-link.tsx`, outside this change, on the invite form, the console's link, /team's "קישור חדש" and the send list, and its fix (a name that stays "העתקת הקישור", a note beside it) changes every one of those buttons and the checks of `team-a11y.spec.ts` (which asserts the focused button named "הקישור הועתק"), `team-invite`, `invite`, `team-pending-links` and `team-import`: a change of its own. (8) A press on its way while the screen crossed from one shape to the other (a 12.9-inch iPad turned, a window resized across 1280 px) put the focus on the page's title and never said the link was ready: `focusOrSay` (new in `@ui/focus-after`, no component) focuses the new link's "וואטסאפ" or copy button, never the title in its place, else says the link is ready, and a focus lost then lands on the title after the words; `SendLinkRow` and `ResendLink` use it. (9–12) Tests the review asked for, below. Tests: `done-links.test.ts` (the expired rows' `name`, null for the nameless one), `focus-after.test.ts` (3 more: what arrived focused and nothing said; drawn in the shape not on screen, said, then the title; the focus put elsewhere, said and left there), `undo-row.test.ts` (a named row's status `aria-atomic="false"`, an unnamed one none). e2e, `design.spec.ts`: the twin's send-list test checks the band below a desktop on "יצירת קישור" and "וואטסאפ" (none on a computer); holds the stand-in's POST with `page.route`, takes the list back while it is held ("ביטול" says six go and one stays), lets it go and sees the words change to five and two, and "ביטול" brings that row back with its link (10); then takes the list back again and lets the hold run out: the line under the field is `removedBack` with `removedKept` 2, and the announcer says nothing of it after the hold (9, 3). A new twin test makes all seven links: no delete, "חזרה לצוות" and "הזמנה מרשימה נוספת" still there (11); then a second list of one new person, whose invite id is none of the first list's, with its "יצירת קישור" and no WhatsApp (12). The failed taking back has the alert and the delete both in view (5). `team-import.spec.ts`: taken back after two links, "אדם אחד יוזמן" is not said within 1.5 s (4). Not added: the review's optional run-out in `team-import.spec.ts`, which would change the later /team steps (the twin covers the screen, pgTAP the database's counts). `pnpm lint:fix`, `pnpm check` (5394 tests). - On chat/import-console from 13487fcc, the head of chat/import-db (part 3, since squash-merged as #271, fce2feaa, on #270, and main merged in here after the check; it held main f84bb68, with parts 1 and 2 and #269). The plan's PR 4 of section 7 ("הוספת אנשים מרשימה בקונסולה"), under Orel's decision of 25.11.2026 that the ConceptApp team pastes first, in the console, at setup: the console's route, its actions and their tests, the console's words, the links to it, the card for a demo, the done card with its undo, the console twin and the e2e. No migration, no SQL, no new npm package, no new `packages/ui` component (so no /design gallery entry). Nothing of PR 5's: no `/team/import`, no send list, no entry rows on /team or /team/invite.
- **The route**, `app/(admin)/admin/businesses/[slug]/people/import/`: `page.tsx` (notFound without `staff.invite` or for an unknown slug; a business closed to invites, `invitesOpen`, gets `ConsoleImportClosed` from `closed-view.tsx`: the console's title, an `EmptyState` "לעסק הזה לא מזמינים אנשים" with `admin.setup.invite.errors.demo` and the way back, no form; else `consoleKnownTeam`, `getGivableNamedRoles` and the business's words for the built-in roles, read with `getWordingForTeam` and `wordingFrom` + `translator(…, "app.roles")`, so the team pastes the words the business uses), `console-roles.ts` (`CONSOLE_GIVER`, an owner with no perms, and `consoleImportRoles`: `importRolesOf` over `inviteChoices` for that giver, managers, workers and the named roles on them, none blocked), and `actions.ts`: `importConsolePeopleAction({id, slug}, input)` and `undoConsoleImportAction({id, slug}, importId)`. Both check `platformCan(staff.role, "staff.invite")` (`noPermission`), then Zod at the boundary: the bound business as a strict `{id, slug}` (an id `isUuid` takes, the slug rule; else `changed`), the rows with `peopleImportRows` (`changed`, nothing asked, nothing logged), then each row as "הוספת אדם" would take it (`parseTeamInvite` for an owner against the live named roles, the same name, number, role and named role back; a named role no longer live is `roleGone`, anything else `changed`), then `importPeopleFromConsole`, whose refusals keep their words (`elsewhere` with the rows' places, `tooManyOpen` with `open` when the database gave it, `demo`, `roleGone`, `tooMany`, `changed`, `failed`); a throw is `failed`, its message alone logged. Done, `revalidatePath` of the business page and `refresh()`, and the answer is `{ok, importId, rows: [{i, status, invite}]}`, rebuilt key by key, so no link or token can ride along. The undo: `isUuid` on the id, `undoConsoleImport`, `{ok, cancelled, kept}`.
- **The shared view**, `app/(app)/team/import/import-view.tsx`, now takes `mode: "team" | "console"` (team by default; `ImportMode` in `import-lines.tsx`). The console's words come from `admin.peopleImport`, never the business's "team" wording group: the title and subtitle, the one-by-one line and problem ("ב„הוספת אדם”"), an expired invite renewed from the business's people ("חידוש ההזמנה"), the done card's `done.console` in place of `done.team` and `done.later`, its `removedKept` and `removeFailed` (the owners delete on /team; the team retries). In console mode the done card is followed by "ההזמנות מהרשימה": everyone the database made or updated, by its answer, never the preview's, with the number in one `ltr` piece and the role as a badge (accent above a worker): a `PersonList` on a phone and a tablet, a `DataTable` (שם | נייד | תפקיד) on a computer. No link is made there. A refusal `elsewhere` maps the rows' places back to the lines sent and their numbers, kept in a set the check reads (`againstTeam`'s new third argument), so each such line is to fix ("המספר כבר בצוות של עסק אחר"), whatever is edited around it, until it is removed; the alert says how many and that an accountant joins from "הוספת אדם", and the first one's "הסרת השורה" takes the focus, since the press is held. Because the console's actions read the page again (`refresh()`), a second list ("הזמנה מרשימה נוספת") is read against the invites the first one made; while an import taken back is held for its "ביטול", its invites are left out of the team the screen reads (by id, for as long as the page hasn't read it again), so its lines read as new at once, not as "כבר ברשימת ההזמנות". Done is built once (`doneOf`) for an import and for the twin's opening states.
- **@domain/people-import.** `PeopleProblem` gains `elsewhere`; `againstTeam(parsed, known, elsewhere = new Set())` turns a line it would write (new or an update) whose number is in the set into that problem, with its name, role and pair kept; a line left out is never one, as the database asks only what it writes.

- **The locked file (holders:317).** At 15:01 the tab's own look hides the file, the focus on "פרטים מזהים" noted (`hold`), and answered open `focusWhenDrawn` puts it back. Since #256 (transitions 0s with less motion) that first try lands at once, but the function still tried again on the page clock's second frame (16 to 32 ms on) and after its 1 s `late`, each putting the focus back whenever it was lost. The test's blur comes three quick calls after the heading's `toBeVisible` (Playwright polls at 0, 20, 50, 100 ms), so now and then inside that window: the focus went back to the title (on that frame, or as `fastForward("14:01")` fired `late`), and `keep.focus()` was a move from it (`moved` → `active` → `due(true)`, over a minute since the last check), which sent `keepFileOpenAction(slide)` with `ca_file_open` still there. `keepFileOpen` sealed the cookie again, the one the test then clears; either its answer moved the tab's 15 minutes on, or the 01:00 lapse's read waited behind it in Next's action queue and read the new cookie. `onClosed` never ran. The failing runs took 7.5 s (desktop) and 8.5 s (phone), about 3 s to line 361 and then the 5 s wait: no slow runner. The function broke its own word ("until it lands"), and nothing else puts the focus back: a read only reads the cookie, only a slide seals it again, and only activity slides.
- **Form 101 (employee-file.spec.ts:738, phone).** `FileSection` drew its "למה מבקשים את זה" row after its children, and every step's `StepDock` (an `h-36` or `h-28` spacer and the fixed dock) was among them, so the button sat below the room kept for the dock: about 124 px above the screen's bottom at the end of the page (`pb-5` and the shell's 6.5rem), where the dock with the tab bar full covers from 88 px up to about 140, or 185 to 230 with a notice of one to three lines. Whether the click landed hung on whether the bar had shrunk, and the hand-in's refresh shortens the page, which can leave it full. The call log: "element is not stable", then an `li` of "ניווט ראשי", then for about 25 s the dock's notice and its row of buttons (`div.pointer-events-auto` in StepDock's `div.xl:hidden`). ## 2026-11-26 · chat · inviting from a list (הזמנה מרשימה), part 1: the reader and the screen, on /design

- On chat/import-reader from origin/main 0578e09e (#265). The plan's PR 1 (section 7), in its smaller form after Orel's decisions of 25.11.2026 (both doors before the pilot, the console first; each person's own link at the moment of sending; the app sends nothing to anyone): the reader, the words the team mode draws, the shared view and its twin. No migration, no SQL, no server action, no route under `app/(app)` (the view lives in `app/(app)/team/import/` without a `page.tsx`), no new package, no new `packages/ui` component (so no /design gallery entry). `SendLinkRow`, `whatsappToHref`, the /team twin's pending rows, the send list's twin state, the console twin and its `admin.peopleImport.*` words are PR 2's and PR 4's, as the plan's "Smaller than before" says.
- **The reader, `@domain/people-import`.** `parsePeopleList(input, roles, fallback)`: past 200,000 characters `tooLong` and nothing read; digits as 0–9 first (Arabic-Indic and Extended Arabic-Indic, `westernDigits`); the delimiter chosen once from most non-empty lines (a tab; else `;` or `,`, whichever more lines hold, a tie to the one written more often, then `;`, read by a hand-written quote-aware split where a quote counts only at a cell's start, `""` is a quote and a quote never closed reads that line as free text; else `|` or a spaced dash, never between digits; else free text); each raw line split before its cells are cleaned, empty cells kept. A header is a row with no mobile before any row with one, holding a header word exactly (`עובד`, `עובדת` and `employee` only beside another) or two cells that name columns; rows before it are `title` lines. Its cells are classified exactly first (the plan's lists, plus `שם עובד/ת`, `טל נייד`), then loosely (`מספר פלאפון`, `שם העובד המלא`, `טלפון הורה`, `מס' ת"ז`), so a second phone header blocks as the plan wants. Two phone columns, headed or by majority, give `phoneColumns` and no lines. With a header the phone comes only from its column (else by majority among the unnamed and unknown ones), the role only from its column (a word there that names no role in `dropped`, the list's role given: after review (10)), the name from the name, or first and last, columns; without one the columns are found by most of their cells, a second text column joins the name only when both hold single words (and, after review (12), it varies and the phone isn't between them), and every column left is named in `ignored` (`{header}` or `{column}`). A phone cell allows Excel's dropped zero; free text needs 0, 972, +972 or 00972. A run that is no mobile is split on its spaces and up to six groups from its start tried; every other run of 5 digits or more, and Excel's `5.01E+08`, go to `dropped`; a word naming the number beside it (`ת.ז`, `נייד:`, `טל'`, `ID`, never `טל` alone) leaves with it. Roles at the end, then the start, longest name first, in the plan's order (the business's names, `builtInRoleWord`, one by one, above rank); a blocked choice gives `givesPay` or `held`. Names through `parseTeamName` (`noName`, `nameTooLong`), `reversed` refused never turned; an initial keeps its dot, a geresh at the end stays. A name line and its number line pair when no line holds both, in the order the paste keeps most. Duplicates by E.164: `sameLine` or `duplicate`; notes `sameNameList` (both lines), `trailingWord` (3 lines and a tenth, free text only), and from `againstTeam` `sameNameTeam`. Past 300 people `tooMany` with no lines, the count kept.
- `againstTeam(parsed, known)`: a member, or an invite with a link (`invited`, `expired`), an owner's or an accountant's (`otherRole`) or one not `touchable` (`invited`), leaves the line out whatever its problem; an invite with no link yet gives `same`, `updateName` (the role it had kept when the line names none), `updateRole` or `update`; `send` is new and updated in line order, `counts.byRole` by the role each ends with, and `blocked` once, in the plan's order. Also `importRolesOf` (staff choices from `inviteChoices` with the business's words, one by one, above rank), `listRoleOf` (workers first), `knownTeamOf` (`waiting`: unclaimed with no link or one still open), `groupOf`, `tableOrder`, and `peopleImportRow(s)` for the server to read again (a name as `parseTeamName` keeps it, `^\+9725\d{8}$`, manager or worker, a uuid or null, 1 to 300, each number once).

Added to the plan's: `summary.leftOutAny` (the folded row counts titles and repeats too), `groups.hide`, `filter.label` and `filter.none`, `removedLines` (a pair), `submit` with `=0` ("הזמנת אנשים", the disabled button before anything can go). `fixLineFor` / `removeLineFor` are "תיקון השורה {line}" / "Fix line {line}" (and remove), so each button's name holds its visible words; the English visible words are "Fix line" and "Remove line".

The status line is the disabled button's description; the counts are said 600 ms after typing stops; a refusal is an alert drawn anew each time and the press takes the focus back. The done card (accent, its title takes the focus, `done.more`, `done.team`, `done.later`, no accent button) offers "מחיקת ההזמנות מהרשימה", held 8 seconds by `useHeldWrite` with an `UndoRow`, back to the editor with the paste and `done.removedBack` (and `removedKept`), "ביטול" bringing the card back with the focus on that button; failing, the card comes back with `errors.removeFailed`.

- After the check (7 findings; 6 fixed, 1 left for before merging): (1) Pressing "הזמנת # אנשים" while an import taken back is still held for its "ביטול" sends that undo first and waits for it (`held.commit()` in `importNow`). Before, the new import went first, found the invites the first one made still waiting and updated them ("לא נוספו הזמנות חדשות"), and then the late undo cancelled them all. When that undo fails, its done card comes back and the new import doesn't go. The twin's stand-ins now keep each import's invites waiting until its undo cancels them, as the database would, each import under its own id, so the twin shows this. (2) A column with no header is named by its place in its own sentence, new `paste.ignoredAt` ("עמודה 3 לא נקלטת.", "עמודות 1 ו-4 לא נקלטות.", en "Column 3 is not read."), beside `paste.ignored` for the columns a header named. `paste.column` is gone: it gave "העמודה עמודה 3 לא נקלטת." A member's line says "כבר בצוות, בשם …" only when the line's own name is known and differs, so the owner's own "בעלים" line says "כבר בצוות". (5) The same number twice is the same person (`sameLine`) when the name and the role are the same, whether the line wrote the role or took the list's: `roleFromLine` no longer makes it a conflict. The first line, which is the one sent, counts as naming its role when either line does, so an invite waiting takes the role a line wrote. (6) The sheet's e2e now checks the whole `paste.ignored` sentence for ת.ז. and שכר לשעה, not just any text with "ת.ז.", which the computer's table also drew in the header line.
